# Changelog

## 0.6.193

- **Fix (#440, the residual window): a clock that steps back under 2 s no longer silences the pump or the TX flows for the step.** A step back landing between a pass's epoch read and its time read gave that pass the new time under the old epoch; the pump's next block then looked due the whole step ahead and the pump waited it out (silent, up to 2 s at 96 kHz, 4 s at 48 kHz), and a TX flow sent nothing for the step. No schedule ever waits that far, so a wait further ahead than W and a block (the pump, the TX pull) or a TX flow's next stamp further ahead than its lag bound (at least the block) and a packet is now a clock gone back: the pump re-bootstraps at once, counted in `rebootstraps_clock_back` (the pump watch line says `cause=clock_back`), and the flow starts again at once (counted, `the TX schedule starts again` with a negative `lag`). RED tests first: steps back of 100 ms and 1 s, on fake time and on the pump thread over a linked follower.
  - When the epoch follows such a pass with the step, the re-anchor keeps the restarted schedule (a one-pass mark, set by the clock-back restart and spent at the next pass's re-anchor point): the step costs one block (pump) or one packet (a TX flow, the TX pull), counted once as a clock gone back, with no second re-bootstrap as `late`, nothing in `missed` and no wake late by the step.
  - Near media time 0 the pump judges a wait from its first block's due time (B + P), so a start right after PTP time 0 with P > W is never taken for a clock gone back.
  - API (vs the consumers' pins): `media::tx::DISCONTINUITY` is removed (nothing else read it). `media::tx::Schedule` gains a public `block: u64` (0 from `Schedule::bootstrap`, set by the TX thread) and `Schedule::ahead_limit()`; a full struct literal adds the field. `Schedule::re_anchor(shift, now)` drops its `block` argument (it reads `Schedule::block`): a caller sets the block on the schedule instead. `Schedule` also gains a `pub(super)` mark (a struct literal outside the crate was already impossible) and a public `Schedule::unmark()`; `stream::Blocks` gains `Blocks::unmark()` and `TxFill::unmark()`.
  - **No latency change.**
- **Fix (#440, the pull): a dlink pull whose clock goes back no longer leaves the consumer's FIFO unfed for the step.** `bridge::pull::due` waited for the clock to come back to where the last read ended, up to the FIFO's reach (0.5 s at 48 kHz). A read leaves the pull at the playout point, so only a clock gone back or a raised P puts it behind; a wait further back than a clock handover's step (`clock::linked::HANDOVER_NS`, 30 µs, rounded up to samples: 2 at 48 kHz, 6 at 192 kHz) now restarts the pull at once at the new playout point, counted in `PullStats::restarts`. A P raised past that room restarts it once too, as the pump re-bootstraps at a new P. RED tests first: re-anchors of 100 ms and 400 ms back; the room's boundary (2 samples back wait, 3 restart at 48 kHz).
  - API (vs the consumers' pins): `bridge::pull::due` takes a fourth argument, `back` (the frames it waits behind), and `bridge::pull::back_room(rate)` gives it. `RxPull::new` is unchanged.
  - **No latency change.**

## 0.6.192

- **Fix, urgent (#440, a gen3 in-ear mixer, iemmixer-snv): the engine pump no longer sleeps through a clock re-anchor.** On 16.11.2026 13:25:43 CET the service clock re-anchored by -43.9 days after a grandmaster with another time base came up, and the pump ran no block for three days, with late, missed and rebootstraps all 0, until the engine was restarted on 19.11. The pump computed its wake from its own time read and the sleep read the time base again, so a re-anchor between the two (a new k of the service, a step of the in-process follower, a source switch) stretched the sleep by the whole step back. The sleep is now bounded by the caller's own read (`PtpClock::sleep_until_within`), so the next pass sees the re-anchor and moves the schedule on the block grid (logged) or re-bootstraps it (counted) within one block. The bug is in every release since the linked clock, v0.6.137 through v0.6.191. The TX thread and the TX relay thread now sleep the same bounded way: a re-anchor before their sleep could stop every TX flow the same way (on 16.11 it did not, by chance). RED tests first; the field record is replayed from `tests/fixtures/pump_stall_440.json`.
  - New warning: `engine pump stalled: no block ran while the clock is ready`, once per stall, when the clock is ready and the pump's blocks did not rise for longer than three blocks or W and a block (judged at the daemon's 250 ms tick), with the blocks, how long, the block's time and the counts. Recovery logs `engine pump runs blocks again after a stall`. Before, `health` read `pump_stalled` and nothing was logged.
  - API (additive): `PtpClock::sleep_until_within(ptp_ns, max_ns)`, a provided method (an app's own clock needs nothing new); `MonoClock`, the linked clock and `ManualClock` override it.
  - Still open (#440): a step back under ~2 s at 96 kHz landing in the ~100 ns between the epoch read and the time read can still silence the pump for up to 2-4 s before a counted re-bootstrap; the clock-back rule closes it next.
  - **Every consumer takes this release** (the weekly sync carries it; iemmixer at once).
- **Fix (#393): dstack-asio judges a from-ASIO underrun's cause against the target less the side's own floor**, half the driver period (inside #422's measured floor: 0.75 ms held / 0.625 ms dropped at 128 frames, 96 kHz), instead of 3/4 of the target. At dsg's 1 ms target with a 128-frame driver, two callbacks 500-750 us late now read `cause: the target under the side's floor plus the callbacks' lateness`, no longer `the bridge (no callback late enough)`. The driver's cause stays for a gap over two periods and for a lateness that alone reaches the target. API: `bridge::Cause::Target` (a `match` on `Cause` adds an arm). The bridge-figures wire is unchanged: `Cause::Target` goes out as `UnderrunCause::Driver` (code 2). Some lines read differently at the same counts: at a 1.333 ms target with a 128-frame driver, 0.75-1.0 ms late was the bridge's and 1.0-1.25 ms the driver's; both are now the target's.
- **No latency change.**

## 0.6.191

- **API (songplayer#324): the control-port slots move into dlink.** `dlink::slots` (slot `k` = ARC `4440 + k`, CMC `8800 + k`, flows `4455 + k`, process id `k`, the first slot whose ports are free on the address and then on the wildcard; `SLOTS`, `slot`, `first_free`, `binds_with`, `pick`, `pick_when_there`) was `dstack_endpoint::slots`, which pulls cpal. An app that embeds dlink alone (SongPlayer's native Dante output) now uses it instead of a copy. `dstack_endpoint::slots` stays as a re-export, so dsend, dlisten and their consumers are unchanged. A pure move, tests included.
  - **No latency change.**
- **Dependency (#435): dsync-ptp v1.35.13 -> v1.35.14.** dsync#204's last part for the in-process follower: the excursion boost decides on a plain-PI mirror, so two followers on one PC stay within 82 ns at a thermal ramp's release (was 7.25 µs), and thermal and 6 ppm / 5 min ramps on 1 s windows stay under 50 µs. No API change. **No latency change.**

## 0.6.190

- **Logs (#427): the TX restart line names its flow slot.** `the TX schedule starts again` now carries `slot` (the flow's TX lane) first, before `lag`, `max_lag` (the bound it was judged by: ours, or that flow's receiver's stated latency when longer, #426), `oversleep_us` and the pass times. A reader of a sender with several flows can match each line to its own receiver. The bridge tests' verdict (`tx_restart/restarts.rs`, shared by tx_pull, tx_pull_counts, rx_pull, from_asio_pull, to_asio_pull) now judges each line by its own lane's receiver latency, never by the shortest of all.
  - API (vs the consumers' pins): `media::tx::Schedule` gains a public `slot: usize` (0 from `Schedule::bootstrap`); a full struct literal adds it.
  - **No latency change.**
- **Tests (#436): `dlink/tests/rx_pull_held.rs`'s verdict names the sender.** A sender's pump restart after a late wake sends silence in whole packets that no RX counter sees; the verdict read that `OURS` (plain at dev1 load ~24: 8 of 9 runs). The judged window now reads the sender's pump restarts, the silence its TX lanes sent and its TX restarts with their lines, and names `SENDER` only with that evidence and the bridge's own counts clean; the asserts are unchanged. Proved under `chrt -f 10`: a sender pump stall read `SENDER`, a dropped pull `OURS`, a late TX sleep `SENDER`.
- Tests (#436): the shared consumer witness (dlink `rx_pull`, dstack-asio `to_asio_pull`) reads a sender's late pump restart as `SENDER`: the window reads the sender's pump restarts and the silence its TX lanes sent (counts opened 50 ms before the window, since the silence plays that much later), and bounds the samples off by that silence + 32 a gap. Holes alone keep the #401 rule. The asserts are unchanged.

## 0.6.189

- **Dependency (#435): dsync-ptp moves from v1.33.1 to v1.35.13.** For every device on the in-process follower (the default): dsync#204 tracks a thermal frequency ramp fast enough (resolume-pp, 18.11.2026: ~6 ppm in minutes after its GPU load started; dsync's simulation ~100 -> ~35 µs), and dsync#206 handles the Windows boot race in dsync-ptp too (dstack's own retry, #431, stays). No API change for dstack's consumers. **No latency change.**
- Test: `daemon::media::tests::the_fixed_*_ports_are_bound_before_any_port_the_os_chooses` skips a base that another test thread's wildcard socket took between its probe and the start (PR #437's coverage run); its expectation is unchanged.

## 0.6.188

- **Fix (#434, PR #432's windows-2025 run): a flow request that came while our TX clock turned ready is answered.** The flow server read the TX clock's readiness twice for one request: once to answer (not ready: left unanswered) and again to decide whether to hold it until the clock is ready. A TX pass that turned the clock ready between the two reads left the request neither granted nor held, so a receiver that asks once (`flows::client::Client::request_flow`, dlink's `flow_start` test) timed out and a real receiver lost its retry interval. Both now decide on one read: a request that comes before the clock is ready is always held, and granted at the next expiry tick (within 100 ms) that reads it ready. Older than v0.6.188 (the server is unchanged since before v0.6.150); seen most right after a device's start, before its TX thread's first pass. RED test first (`flows/server/tests/ready_race.rs`). **No latency change, no API change.**
- **Fix (#433, v0.6.187's tag CI on windows-2025): dlink's fill-the-room output no longer re-centres and underruns after a held callback.** A fill-the-room output (dlisten on Windows, `Player::fill_pulled`) held to just past a tick, less late past it than its usual calls are past theirs (a timer that wakes every sleep about 1 ms late): the gap's dry call drops a period under the card's silence there, and the player took that period for an excess (1 underrun, 1 re-centre, 5088 frames dropped for 5568 of silence). It now makes no controller observation while the gap is open, and the settling drops the rest. RED test first; `dlink/tests/rx_pull_held.rs` gains a fourth case (every wake 1 ms late, the hold to 0.9 ms past a tick), and its holds come every 71 events so they meet all four phases of the converter's blocks against the 480-frame calls. **No latency change.**
- **Fix (#431, mbc-snv 19.11.2026): an in-process follower start that failed at boot is tried again.** The follower (dsync-ptp) failed to start 4 s after logon, before Npcap had a device for the pinned adapter ("no Npcap device"), and was never tried again; with dsync's service hitting the same race (dsync#206), the device had no media clock and dropped every flow request until a manual dsync restart. A start that fails for a cause that can pass (no Npcap device for the adapter yet, the adapter without an IPv4 yet, the capture not opened) is now tried again on the selector's round: 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, then every 30 s, forever. The device runs on the dsync service meanwhile. Each try after the first is seeded from the poller's last service reply (its frequency word when locked and settled), so a late start does not run unseeded.
  - Lines: the boot failure warns once (`media clock: dsync's in-process follower did not start yet: the dsync service's clock is used, its start is tried again`, `retry_in_s`, `start_ms`). While the tries run, the once-a-minute line says `reason=stopped` with `start_attempts`, `retrying_for_s` and the last error. After 600 s still failing, ONE WARN: `media clock: dsync's in-process follower has failed to start for 10 minutes: the dsync service's clock is still used, its start is tried again every 30 s`. The try that starts it logs `... follower started` with `start_attempts`, `retrying_for_s` and `seed_ppb`, and the selector then takes it the usual way (agree, then hand over).
  - Status: `clock.follower.waiting` = `stopped` while trying; clock figures (#429) `follower` 1, `follower_waiting` 1.
  - A cause that cannot pass (Npcap not installed, port 319 refused, no adapter of that name) is unchanged: one warning, no follower.
  - API: `clock::linked::start_internal` is removed (only the host called it); `Linked::start(service, Starter::dsync(iface, seed))` starts the follower and keeps trying. New public in `clock::linked`: `Starter` (+ `reseeded_from`), `Attempt`, `attempt`, `failed`, `start_passes`, `retry_delay_s`, `RETRY_MAX_S`, `seed_now`, `ESCALATE_AFTER_S`, `STILL_NOT_STARTED_LINE`.
  - **No latency change.**

## 0.6.187

- **Feature (#429, for dcontroller#124): clock figures on the info port.** Every dstack device answers a dstack-own request (`07 xx d5 b3`, reply `07 2a d5 b2`, 66 bytes, version 1, big-endian, unicast to the asker, at most 10 a second) with its media clock's state: the source (none / in-process follower / dsync service / test system clock), readiness; the in-process follower's locked, settled and holdover flags, error p90 (ns), offset from the service while a switch waits to agree (ns), frequency (ppb), holdovers, grandmaster and wait reason (#420's words); the dsync service's last poll result, locked/settled/holdover, mode, frequency word (ppb), reply age (ms), holdovers and grandmaster. "Not known" is `i64::MIN` / `u32::MAX` / a zero UUID on the wire and `None` in the struct. No app hand-over: the stack knows its clock.
  - Reader: `info::client::clock_figures(&sock, ip, Some(device_id), wait) -> Result<ClockFigures, InfoError>` (`ClockFigures { sequence, source: TimeSource, ready, follower: Option<FollowerFigures>, service: Option<ServiceFigures> }`). `Timeout` = a non-dstack device, a dstack before this release, or an older app holding the address's 8700; `Malformed` = a wrong version/length or a code outside its table.
  - Several devices on one address answer through the share manager; a manager before this release does not pass the request on, so every dstack app on such a PC takes this release before a controller relies on it.
  - API (vs the consumers' pins): `clock::FollowerCounts` gains `freq_ppb` and `offset_from_service_ns` (a full struct literal adds them); `ClockStats` gains `set_poll` / `service`.
  - dsync's `ptp_phase_error_us` is not in it (the DSYX reply the stack reads has none): a controller reads that from a service node's `:8898/status` over the LAN.
- **CI: the Linux jobs' apt step installs from the last good run's packages.** The runner's Ubuntu mirrors and the fallback mirrors.edge.kernel.org both failed on 18-19.11.2026 (dev push run 44407677291, the v0.6.186 release's Linux binaries 44423972765). `actions/cache` (v6.1.0, pinned) keeps the debs of the last good install, keyed on the runner image and `scripts/ci-apt-deps.sh`; a hit installs them with `dpkg -i` and needs no mirror. A miss walks the runner's mirrors, then kernel.org, Princeton and RWTH Aachen, each try's update and download bounded to 240 s (the install itself is not: a limit that stopped dpkg left its lock held, push run 44428444113).
- Playbook: WPR DPC/ISR vs a send queue on a Windows PC, and a QoS throttle must match the flow's protocol (#425).
- **No latency change.**

## 0.6.186

- **Fix (#426): a TX flow holds on for its receiver's latency, not only ours.** A flow's TX schedule restarted once its lag passed OUR advertised latency, even when its receiver states a longer one, and plays the flow at that longer latency (a receiver plays at max(its own, ours), the rule `PassClock::late_threshold` already counts by). So a TX thread waking late by more than our latency, yet within the receiver's, threw away packets the receiver would still have played. Seen in v0.6.185's dev CI (windows-2025): a 96 kHz sender advertising 8.36 ms, a receiver stating 13.67 ms, a sleep returning 8.47 ms late, 27 packets (864 samples) dropped, `over_latency` 0. A flow now restarts past `Schedule::bound` = max(our latency, its receiver's stated latency), capped at the engine ring's reach (`device::MAX_LATENCY_SAMPLES`); multicast and a receiver not yet known keep ours. The restart line's `max_lag` names the bound it was judged by. The TX pull's catch-up reach takes the flows' largest bound, so the fill never restarts before them. A re-anchor's room stays on our latency.
  - Tests: RED `media/tx/tests/receiver_bound.rs` first. The bridge tests' verdict (`dlink/tests/tx_restart/restarts.rs`) now reads a restart bound under the receiver's latency as OURS, and `tx_pull.rs` / `tx_pull_counts.rs` wait for the sender to learn its receiver's latency before judging.
  - **No latency change, no API change.** A sender now sends late packets that a longer-latency receiver can still use.
- Docs: `docs/two-networks.md`: any `ethtool -C` change on a running `r8152` link stops and restarts the MAC (~2 s with no Dante RX, TX EAGAIN, `rx_missed` back to 0; same ifindex, dstack resumes by itself), and the RTL8156 default `rx-usecs` 15000 is ns (#408). Playbook: a receiver's "late" split into send-side and after-send (#424), pktmon on Windows and a USB RTL8156's one send queue (#425).

## 0.6.185

- **Feature (#422, dasio#190, the owner's directive of 18.11.2026 to lower dsg's bridge delay to the lowest stable value): dstack-asio's per-direction targets go down to 0.5 ms.** dlink's app-buffer floor of 1 ms (`MIN_APP_BUFFER_NS`) was a sanity bound set in #247 for dlink apps, not a measured limit of the ASIO bridge, and `AppBuffer` clamped anything lower back to 1 ms. Measured in simulation at 96 kHz (Fifo + Player on simulated time, both sides pulled, 180 s a run): to-ASIO holds from 0.5 ms cold (0.125 ms once seeded); from-ASIO with a 128-frame driver needs 0.75 ms seeded plus the driver's callback lateness (1.0 ms late needs 1.0 ms), with a 64-frame driver 0.25-0.375 ms. So `to_asio_app_buffer_ns` / `from_asio_app_buffer_ns` now take 0.5 to 150 ms (`dstack_asio::bridge::MIN_BRIDGE_TARGET_NS` = 500 000), checked at `Bridge::new` (`direction_target_problem(key, ns)`, text `to_asio_app_buffer_ns: 499999 ns (0.499999 ms); 0.5 to 150 ms`); a direction's own target plays unclamped. The shared `app_buffer_ns`, and every dlink app, keep 1 ms.
  - API (additive): dlink `AppBuffer::with_floor`, `floor_ns`, `problem_for(key)`; dstack-asio `MIN_BRIDGE_TARGET_NS`, `direction_target_problem`. dtray's dasio settings window takes the same per-direction range.
  - **dasio:** check the two per-direction keys with `direction_target_problem` (keep dlink's `AppBuffer::problem` for `app_buffer_ns`); step dsg's to-ASIO target down from 1.0 ms toward 0.5 ms, and its from-ASIO target to no less than 0.75 ms + its callback lateness. A lower target is a lower latency by the same amount.
- **Diagnostics (#420): the in-process PTP follower says why it is not in use.** While dsync's in-process follower is not the selected media clock source, one INFO line a minute: `media clock: the in-process follower is not in use reason=<stopped|no_ptp|not_locked|not_settled|waits_to_agree> windows_last_minute=.. grandmaster=.. locked=.. settled=.. error_ns=.. offset_ns=.. source=..`, and the status key `clock.follower.waiting` (null while in use). API: `clock::FollowerCounts` gains `grandmaster` and `waiting` (a full struct literal adds them).
  - **No latency change** for any dlink app; a dasio bridge's latency moves only when dasio lowers its per-direction targets.

## 0.6.184

- **Feature (#419, for dcontroller#117): bridge figures body version 2: the through-latency each way and the ASIO driver's name.** Appended to version 1's 120 bytes, unchanged: `asio_to_dante_us` (u32 at 0x78), `dante_to_asio_us` (u32 at 0x7c), µs, 0 = not known; `driver_len` (u8 at 0x80) and the driver's name (63 bytes UTF-8 at 0x81, cut at a char boundary); 192 bytes. A reply is always version 2; `parse_figures` / `client::bridge_figures` read version 1 (120 bytes, the new fields `None`) and version 2.
  - **Rollout order:** a reader before this release (dcontroller v0.1.75) refuses a version 2 reply as `Malformed`, so **dcontroller takes this release first**, then dasio sets the values. Several devices on one address: a manager of this release hands over a member's reply of either version, a manager before it drops a member's version 2 reply, so **every dstack app on a dasio PC takes this release**.
  - API: `BridgeFigures` gains `asio_to_dante_us: Option<u32>`, `dante_to_asio_us: Option<u32>`, `driver: Option<String>` (a full struct literal adds them) and is no longer `Copy` (still `Clone`); new `FigureSlot::set_path(asio_to_dante_us: u32, dante_to_asio_us: u32)` and `FigureSlot::set_driver(name: &str)` (like `set_underruns`: the next reply carries the last values); `bridge::VERSION` is now 2, new `VERSION_1`, `FIGURES_LEN_V2` (192), `DRIVER_MAX` (63), `driver_cut`; `figures_body` returns `[u8; FIGURES_LEN_V2]`; `FiguresError` gains `DriverLength(u8)` and `DriverText` (an exhaustive match adds the arms).
  - **dasio:** `media.bridge_figures().set_path(asio_to_dante_us, dante_to_asio_us)` and `.set_driver(name)` (µs; 0 / empty = not known). **dcontroller:** read the three new fields. **No latency change.**

## 0.6.183

- **Fix (#414, #409's family): the Linux socket past the qdisc (AF_PACKET, a device with CAP_NET_RAW) follows a NIC re-create.** It names its interface by index in every send, and that index was read once at open; after a re-create (same name and address, new index) every direct send failed with ENXIO and the unicast flows stayed dead until a restart (reproduced in two netns: 60 s, the receiver at 0 packets). The index is now an atomic the TX thread loads per send (no lock, no allocation), moved by the #411 interface watch (`the direct socket sends on the interface's new index`); until the watch's next look (at most 2 s) sends fail and are counted. CI's `recreate` job now runs two netns joined by a veth pair, so a peer takes the direct path, and requires it to rise again. Windows' host frame sender has the same pattern but falls back to UDP (nothing lost): #417.
- **Fix (#417): Windows' host frame sender follows a NIC re-create.** `Media::set_frame_sender` read our NIC's index once; after a re-create (same address, new index) every neighbour row was filtered out and the flows fell back to UDP until the app restarted (nothing lost, but the kernel send path, dasio's filter, unused). The TX interface watch now moves the host path's index too (`TxShared::follow_host`, one INFO line a move) and takes each slot's MAC again. API (additive): `Membership::keep_with` (the watch hands its change to the action; `keep_as` unchanged).
- **CI: an apt try that runs out of 4 min moves to the fallback mirror**, and the fallback covers every `*.archive.ubuntu.com` host: a runner's us-east-2.ec2 mirror took 9-10 min for the step and the fmt job hit its 10 min limit twice.
  - **No latency change.** **Consumers:** move the pin with Wednesday's sync; nothing to port.

## 0.6.182

- **Fix (#411, #409's follow-up): a device's own multicast TX flows send again after its NIC is re-created.** Each TX flow socket set its multicast send interface once at bind, and Linux keeps the interface INDEX: after a re-create (a USB reset, a new ifindex) every multicast send failed with ENODEV for good (in the netns proof: 60 s of `os_error=19`, the receiver at 0 packets). The daemon's tick now keeps a second interface watch beside #282's RX one and names every flow socket's send interface again on a change (`multicast send interface named again ... the TX flow sockets`). Not covered yet: the Linux AF_PACKET TX bypass socket (#414).
- **CI (#411): a whole-device interface re-create test** (`recreate` job): two `dstack-dev peer` devices in a netns on a dummy `rig0`, one receiving the other by multicast and the other by unicast; `rig0` is deleted for 5 s and re-added with a new index, and both must deliver again within 60 s.
- **Docs (#407): a Dante leg on a netplan/systemd-networkd host** keeps its real prefix (the kernel's prefix route stays in main there), never a `/32`: dstack's devices report their interface netmask to Dante Controller, so a `/32` reads as a subnet mismatch.
  - **No latency change.** **Consumers:** move the pin with Wednesday's sync; nothing to port.

## 0.6.181

- **Fix (#409): a device finds its transmitters again after its NIC is re-created.** On stream-snv (18.11.2026) the Dante NIC (a USB RTL8156B) went through a USB reset and came back under the same name with a new interface index; the running stream-obs (dlink in OBS) never received again (`unknown_device` every minute) until OBS was restarted, 23 min later. Cause: the RX directory's mDNS query socket and discovery's sweeper set their multicast send interface once at start, and Linux keeps the interface INDEX, so every later query failed with ENODEV (logged only at debug). Now the #282 interface watch also names the send interface again when the address returns or its index changes (`multicast send interface named again after an interface change`), failed query sends are counted (`Instances::send_errors`), warned at most once a minute, and three in a row name the interface again. Our own multicast TX flows have the same pattern: #411.
- **Fix (#410): a converting dlink app's `Block::play_at` is d earlier, d = the RX converter's delay.** `Rated` added the converter's delay to the device block's play time, but the converter's output already holds audio that much older, so the stamp counted it twice: 0.625 ms late at 48 <-> 96 kHz, about 1.3 ms at 44.1 kHz. **Not a latency change**: the audio reaches the app exactly as before; only its stamp moves d earlier, to the documented path (the content's wire stamp + P + (c)). An app that places audio by `play_at` (OBS's dante-input) moves its A/V by d; camera-box's A/V expectation moves by 0.625 ms. A fixed app block still adds its FIFO wait.
- **Guard (#403): `play_at` against the audio's wire stamp**, a whole-device test (`dlink/tests/play_at_stamp.rs`): a sender codes each sample's wire stamp into its audio, an obs-shaped receiver (48 kHz app on 96 kHz) checks `play_at` less (stamp + P) = (c) RX (+ the flow's read delay): 1.958 ms at a 9 ms transmitter, 29.958 ms at a 40 ms one.
- **Log (#403): "RX flow granted" carries the flow's `latency`, `playout`, `delay` and `advertised`** (samples), so an app's log shows a transmitter's latency moving its audio (#402: mbc's 40 -> 5 ms read 28 ms earlier at stream-obs, unseen in the log).
- **Tests (#401, #396): the bridge tests name the runner, the sender or the stack.** The TX-pull tests read the sender's TX schedule restarts and their own sleep's `oversleep_us`; `rx_pull.rs` and dstack-asio's `to_asio_pull.rs` share one consumer witness that judges lateness against the schedule (not gaps) and reads never-written ring holes with the sender's late restarts as `SENDER`.
- **Docs (#290): which NIC and driver may carry a Dante leg, measured.** The RTL8156 on Windows keeps the NetAdapterCx driver: Realtek's NDIS driver ran USB DPCs of up to 19.3 ms on mbc (Cx under 0.3 ms). The Linux r8152 needs its RX ring at 4096 and headroom on a shared NIC (#408).
  - **No latency change.** **obs:** move the pin (the weekly sync carries it); your A/V moves 0.625 ms earlier (#410). **Other consumers:** nothing to change.

## 0.6.180

- **The weekly consumer sync asks a consumer to move only when the library code changed (#399).** The first run (18.11.2026) listed all 7 consumers as behind v0.6.179, a release that changed only a workflow and scripts: exactly the per-release churn the owner ruled out. A pin now counts as behind only when the library crates (`dstack`, `dlink`, `dstack-audio-io`, `dstack-resample`, `dstack-endpoint`, `dstack-asio`) or `vendor/mdns-sd` differ between it and the latest tag; otherwise its row reads "current (no library change to vX)". The same read on 18.11: songplayer (main v0.6.99) and iemmixer (v0.6.137) behind, the other five current.
- **CI: the apt step leaves third-party sources out.** The v0.6.179 release's Linux binaries job failed when packages.microsoft.com answered 429 (`apt-get update --error-on=any`), while the Ubuntu mirrors worked; every package the step installs comes from Ubuntu's archive, so `scripts/ci-apt-deps.sh` moves the runner's other sources aside first.
  - **No code or latency change.** Consumers: nothing to change.

## 0.6.179

- **Weekly consumer version sync (#399, the owner's call of 18.11.2026).** Consumers had drifted far behind (intercom 60 releases, iemmixer 41), while an upgrade ask per micro-release costs every consumer session tokens. Now once a week, Wednesday 06:00 UTC (07:00 CET), the `Consumer pins` workflow reads each consumer's pinned dstack tag from its Cargo.lock (`scripts/consumers.toml`) and opens or updates one "Weekly consumer version sync" issue listing those behind the latest release; dstack then sends each its upgrade notes from its own pin. Between Wednesdays a release goes only to the consumer that reported its bug or asked for its feature.
  - **No code or latency change.** **Every consumer:** expect one upgrade ask a week at most (Wednesday), with the API changes from your own pin; nothing to change now.

## 0.6.178

- **Fix (#393): the underrun line judges the top lateness bin by the run's peak lateness.** Since 0.6.177 a callback's lateness is summed over switches that did not catch up, so the top bin (2.0 ms or more behind) can hold callbacks later than one period, the reach it was judged by: 3.5 ms behind at a 4 ms from-ASIO target read as the bridge's. The counters now keep the run's peak lateness behind the driver's schedule (`DeviceEvents::late_max_ns`, `runner::Status::late_max_us`); a check whose peak rose judges the top bin by it, any other by the period as before. dsg's 1.667 ms target is not affected (its top bin is the driver's either way).
  - API (additive): `DeviceEvents::late_max_ns`, `Status::late_max_us`, `UnderrunCounts::late_max_us`, `Counters::late_max_ns`. A full `UnderrunCounts { .. }` or `DeviceEvents { .. }` literal adds the field.
  - **dasio:** move the pin; log `late_max_us` beside `late_bins` in the counts line if wanted. **Other consumers:** nothing to change. **No latency change.**
- **Tests (#395): `dlink/tests/rx_pull.rs` witnesses its output thread.** A windows-2025 run (PR #394) failed it with 2 underruns and no ring hole: the runner not running the test's output thread, as `to_asio_pull.rs` once did. It now records the thread's own gaps, sets aside a window with a stall over two periods (up to 4) and prints `verdict RUNNER|OURS`. Both consumer witnesses guard 100 ms after a set-aside window and count underruns from before a window's first fill.

## 0.6.177

- **Fix (#393, dasio#120): an ASIO bridge's callback lateness is measured against the driver's schedule.** dsg's bridge (MiniFuse, 128 frames at 96 kHz, from-ASIO target 1.667 ms) logged two from-ASIO underruns as `cause: the bridge (no callback late enough)` with only 500-1000 us late callbacks counted. The lateness bins counted each switch's gap minus the period alone. The MiniFuse delivers on 1 ms USB frames (gaps 2, 1, 1 ms: a third of all switches read 500-750 us), and a frame slip (two 2 ms gaps in a row) puts the driver's write 1.0-1.333 ms behind its schedule while the gaps read 500-750 us twice.
  - Each switch is now timed against the driver's schedule line (`dstack_asio::state::schedule_lag`): due one period after the line; one on time or early re-anchors it; a late one creeps it at most a thousandth of a period, so a driver clock slower than the PC's never sums into a lag. A gap over two periods still counts as `late_callbacks` only, and re-anchors the line.
  - The bins keep their keys (`late_250us`..`late_2000us`) and width; they now count how far behind the schedule a switch came, the top one 2.0 ms or more behind. Expect more counts than before: a switch that comes on a shorter gap but has not caught up counts too (dsg: the switch one 1 ms frame after a 2 ms gap is still 0.333 ms behind). The underrun line, its cause and dcontroller's bridge figures read the new counts.
  - API: `dstack_asio::state::lateness_bin` takes the lateness (`late_ns`) instead of `(gap_ns, period_ns)`; new `state::schedule_lag(now_ns, line_ns, period_ns) -> (late_ns, line_ns)`.
  - **No latency change.** **dasio:** move the pin; no code change. **Other consumers:** nothing to change.

## 0.6.176

- **Feature (#391, dcontroller#108): a bridge device's own figures over the network.** dcontroller can now read a dasio bridge device's targets, converter delays, underruns (with each side's latest cause) and callback lateness bins over the Dante network, about once a second, to graph them beside the packet latency. Until now they were in the local control socket's status only.
  - **The read:** a dstack-own request on the info port 8700, opcode `07 xx d5 b1 00 00 00 00` (no Audinate message type uses 0xd5xx), answered by a dstack device that holds bridge figures, UNICAST to the asking socket (reply `07 2a d5 b0 00 00 00 00`), never to the info group 224.0.0.231:8702 or the heartbeat, which Dante Controller and every Audinate device read. Any other device leaves it unanswered, as any opcode it does not know. Bounded like the info replies (10 a second, one bound for every asker; a request over it reads nothing). Several devices on one address: the address's manager relays the request with its sender to its members (new share messages `A` and `U`) and sends a member's reply only as a bridge reply, only to an asker it relayed within the last second, only to one host; a member or manager older than this release ignores the new messages, so that device answers nothing until both run it.
  - **The body, version 1, 120 bytes, big-endian** (`dstack::info::bridge`, whose doc holds the table): `version` (1), `to_asio_cause`, `from_asio_cause` (u8 each: 0 none yet, 1 bridge, 2 driver callback lateness, 3 unknown), `sequence` (u32, each reply's number from 1 at the device's start), `run_start_unix_ms` (u64, when the device's figures started), `to_asio_target_us`, `from_asio_target_us`, `to_asio_converter_delay_us`, `from_asio_converter_delay_us` (u32 each, us; 0 = no driver opened yet), `to_asio_underruns`, `from_asio_underruns` (u64, since the device's start), `late_callbacks` and `late_250us`..`late_2000us` (u64 each, this driver run's; they fall at a driver reopen). A reader refuses another version or length. The targets, delays and late counts are read at each request; the underruns and their causes are the app's last hand-over (dasio's counts tick, every 10 s), so a cause always belongs to the counts beside it.
  - **dcontroller:** `dstack::info::client::bridge_figures(&sock, device_ip, Some(device_id), wait) -> Result<BridgeFigures, InfoError>` with `sock = dstack::info::client::unicast_socket(our_dante_ip)?` (one socket for every device; the reply comes back to it alone). `InfoError::Timeout` from a device without figures (any Audinate device, a dstack device without a bridge, an older dstack, or a dasio bridge whose PC's 8700 is held by an older dstack app); `InfoError::Malformed` for another version. A reply to an earlier ask's resend can come late: drop a `sequence` already seen. `BridgeFigures` names each field by its key above; `client::Ask` gained `BridgeFigures` (a `match` over `Ask` adds the arm).
  - **dasio:** two calls in `run_bridge.rs`, once the device, the bridge and the runner exist: `lock(&shared).publish_figures(media.bridge_figures(), Arc::clone(&status))` (dstack-asio `Bridge::publish_figures`: the targets, converter delays, bins and late callbacks are read live at each request), and on every counts tick right after `underrun_tick`: `underruns.publish(media.bridge_figures())` (dstack-asio `UnderrunWatch::publish`: the underrun counts and each side's latest cause, which `UnderrunWatch::look` now keeps). The device's setter itself is `Media::bridge_figures().set_underruns(to_asio, from_asio, to_cause, from_cause)` (`dstack::info::FigureSlot`). Without these calls the device answers nothing to the request.
  - API: additive `dstack::info::{bridge, BridgeFigures, FigureSlot, OwnFigures, UnderrunCause}`, `info::Beat::bridge` (returns `Option<&FigureSlot>`, default `None`), `info::InfoBuilder::bridge_within`, `info::share::{Asks, is_unicast, is_bridge_reply, ASK_HOLD_NS, MAX_ASKS}`, `Media::bridge_figures`, `info::client::{bridge_figures, unicast_socket}`; dstack-asio `Bridge::publish_figures`, `bridge::own_figures`, `UnderrunWatch::publish`, `From<Cause> for UnderrunCause`. A `match` over `info::Kind`, `info::client::Ask` or `info::share::Msg` adds the new arm(s) (`Kind::BridgeFigures`, `Ask::BridgeFigures`, `Msg::Ask`/`Msg::SendTo`); a `Sources { .. }` literal adds `bridge`; `info::limit::BOUNDED` is now `[Kind; 9]`.
  - **No latency change.** **Other consumers** (obs, camera-box, intercom, songplayer, presenter, iemmixer, dsend, dlisten, dtray): no code change, but an app that shares a PC (one address) with a dasio bridge must take this release for dcontroller's read to reach the bridge: whichever dstack app binds 8700 first relays the address's requests, and an older one relays the bridge request in the old form, which the bridge does not answer.

## 0.6.175

- **Fix (#387): dtray reads a dasio bridge instance that sets `converter_taps`.** dasio's `[bridge]` takes the converter length (dstack-asio `Spec::converter_taps`, v0.6.174); dtray refused the file as an unknown field. It now reads it, refuses one outside 32-256 in steps of 8 (as dasio and dstack-asio do), keeps the file's on an edit and writes a changed one. The settings window shows the keys it has no control for (the per-direction targets and the converter length) in the note beside the app buffer (`file: to 1.333333 ms, from 2 ms, 48 taps`). API (additive): `dasio::bridge::{taps_problem, note_text, MIN_CONVERTER_TAPS, CONVERTER_TAPS, DEFAULT_NOTE}`. No latency change.

## 0.6.174

- **Fix (#387): dtray reads a dasio bridge instance that sets the per-direction buffer targets.** dasio's `[bridge]` table takes `to_asio_app_buffer_ns` and `from_asio_app_buffer_ns` (dstack-asio v0.6.172); dtray's settings window refused such a file as an unknown field ("Settings not readable"). It now reads both, checks their range as the app buffer's, writes a changed one and keeps the file's on an edit (the window has no control for them). No latency change.

- **Feature (#382, the owner's ROZHODNUTÉ of 17.11.2026): an ASIO bridge's converter length is a setting.** The owner's call: the SoundGrid driver runs at 96 kHz too (96 <-> 96, no rate change), and the bridge's converter goes to the minimum to show how low the path can go; production may raise it later.
  - **`dstack_asio::bridge::Spec::converter_taps: Option<u32>`** sets both directions' sinc length (the to-ASIO and from-ASIO players, the idle from-ASIO player before the first open too). `None` is 256 taps, exactly as before. Accepted `MIN_CONVERTER_TAPS` (32) to `MAX_CONVERTER_TAPS` (256) in steps of 8 (the converter core takes multiples of 8 and would round any other length up); anything else refuses `Bridge::new` with `converter_taps: 31; 32 to 256 in steps of 8`.
  - **Measured at 96 <-> 96 kHz** (the crate's own helpers, +7.5 ppm, a -1 dBFS tone 20 Hz-20 kHz): 256 taps 1.333 ms a direction, flat; **48 taps 0.250 ms a direction, flat** (within 5e-7 dB to 20 kHz, THD+N 212 dB): 1.083 ms less each way, nothing lost in the audio band; 32 taps 0.167 ms, -0.40 dB at 20 kHz (-0.24 dB at 19 kHz, -0.02 dB at 15 kHz). With a rate change a short filter cuts the band (#382 issuecomment-6952851834: 128 taps lose 0.9 dB at 20 kHz at 96 -> 48 kHz), so keep 256 (or 192) there.
  - **dlink (additive):** `Player::with_taps(taps)` (right after `Player::new`; refused for a length the converter refuses) and `Stats::converter_delay_ns()` (the current player's converter delay at its output rate: 1333333 ns at 96 <-> 96 kHz with 256 taps, 250000 with 48). **dstack-resample (additive):** `Converter::drifting_with_taps(.., taps)`, `TAPS_STEP`.
  - **No latency change** for any consumer that does not set it: every default stays (256 taps). A dasio bridge with `converter_taps = 48` moves its latency by -1.083 ms each way at 96 <-> 96 kHz.
  - Each consumer:
    - **dasio:** `Spec` gained `converter_taps`: add `converter_taps: None` to its `Spec { .. }`, or set it from a new `[bridge]` key; dsg runs `Some(48)` (and may show `Some(32)` for the how-low test). Log `bridge.to_asio_stats().converter_delay_ns()` / `from_asio_stats()` with the targets if the status should carry each direction's converter delay.
    - **dtray:** its dasio settings refuse unknown `[bridge]` keys: a new dasio key for the length needs dtray to know it first (with dasio's key name).
    - **obs, camera-box, intercom, songplayer, presenter, iemmixer, dsend, dlisten:** nothing to change.

## 0.6.173

- **Feature (#386, dasio#120): dstack-asio bins the driver callback's lateness under two periods, and names it at each underrun.** A callback counted `late` only past a gap of two periods (more than 1.333 ms late at 128 @ 96 kHz), while the from-ASIO side loses audio from 1.0-1.5 ms late at a 1.333 ms target (#382's measurement): dasio saw from-ASIO underruns with `late_callbacks` 0.
  - **Lateness bins:** each buffer switch 0.25 ms late or more, up to a gap of two periods, counts in one of eight 0.25 ms bins of its lateness (gap less the period): 0.25-0.5, 0.5-0.75, ..., 1.75-2.0 ms, and 2.0 ms or more. Past two periods it counts in `late_callbacks` only, as before (no switch counted twice); under 0.25 ms in none. Decided by the pure `state::lateness_bin(gap_ns, period_ns)`; counted per run like `late_callbacks` (`engine::Counters::late_bins`, `DeviceEvents::late_bins`, `runner::Status::late_bins`, a `[u64; 8]`).
  - **New status keys (dasio, additive):** one per bin, `state::LATE_BIN_KEYS` = `late_250us`, `late_500us`, `late_750us`, `late_1000us`, `late_1250us`, `late_1500us`, `late_1750us`, `late_2000us` (each the bin's lower edge; the same keys at every buffer size: at 128 @ 96 kHz the bins from 1.5 ms on stay 0, a callback later than 1.333 ms being `late_callbacks`). The existing keys (`late_callbacks`, `fast_callbacks`, `gap_max_ms`, ...) are unchanged.
  - **The underrun line:** `bridge::UnderrunWatch::look(UnderrunCounts::of(&status, to_asio_underruns, from_asio_underruns), from_stats.target_ms())`, from the app's status loop (dasio's 10 s counts tick), WARNs when a player's underruns rose since the last check, e.g. `asio bridge underruns since the last check: from-ASIO 2 (target 1333 us), cause: driver callback lateness; driver callbacks late meanwhile: 1000-1250 us: 1`. A from-ASIO underrun reads as the driver's when a callback came over two periods late or surely at least 3/4 of the from-ASIO target late (a bin's lower edge; at 1.333 ms: from 1.0 ms late; the open 2.0 ms bin is judged by the driver's period, as late as its callbacks can be: `UnderrunCounts::period_us`, from the status's rate and buffer), else as the bridge's (`cause: the bridge (no callback late enough)`); `cause: unknown (no target yet)` while the from-ASIO target reads 0. A to-ASIO one reads as the driver's only past two periods (its pull and play share the switch). `bridge::{from_asio_cause, to_asio_cause, Cause}` are the pure decisions. A count that falls (a new run, a new player) counts again from 0. asio-probe prints the eight bins too.
  - **No latency change:** no target, block, converter or default moves; the bins are counters in the driver callback (atomics, no allocation).
  - Each consumer:
    - **dasio:** `bridge_json` adds the eight keys from `status.late_bins` with `LATE_BIN_KEYS`; keep an `UnderrunWatch` in the instance loop and call `look` on the counts tick. A struct literal of `DeviceEvents` or `runner::Status` adds `late_bins` (or uses `..Default::default()`).
    - **songplayer, obs, camera-box, intercom, presenter, iemmixer, dsend, dlisten:** nothing to change.

## 0.6.172

- **Feature (#382, dasio#120): an ASIO bridge's directions take their own targets, and each ASRC starts from the last run's locked rate.** dasio measured on a 96 kHz bridge (128-frame driver buffer, pump block 32) that one `app_buffer_ns` for both directions cannot go lower: at 1.333 ms the to-ASIO side held (0 underruns with 4 late driver callbacks) and the from-ASIO side did not (4 underruns in 29 min).
  - **Why from-ASIO needs more** (measured, #382): a late driver callback is a late write into the from-ASIO FIFO while the TX thread reads on time, so that side's target must cover the driver's lateness one for one; the to-ASIO pull and play share the buffer switch, so a late switch costs it nothing. A simulation with the stack's own `Fifo`/`Player` (±7.5 ppm, from 90 s one late callback every 7 s) held a callback 1.0 ms late at 1.333 ms (1.25 ms at +7.5 ppm) and lost audio from 1.25 ms late (-7.5 ppm) or 1.5 ms (+7.5 ppm). dstack-asio counts a callback `late` only past a gap of two periods (2.667 ms at 128 @ 96 kHz, i.e. more than 1.333 ms late), so a callback 1.0-1.33 ms late drains the from-ASIO FIFO unseen, which explains dasio's underruns "with no late callback". The from-ASIO converter's 128-frame calls are not the cause: 32-frame calls at the same target hold less (they cut 0.7-1.2 ms of latency) and at the same latency hold about the same, so the call size is unchanged.
  - **Per-direction targets:** `dstack_asio::bridge::Spec` has `to_asio_app_buffer_ns` and `from_asio_app_buffer_ns` (`None`: `app_buffer_ns`, which stays the default for both; `None` there too: the ASIO buffer + one pump block, as before). `Bridge::to_asio_app_buffer` / `Bridge::from_asio_app_buffer` give each direction's target (`Bridge::app_buffer` stays the shared one); a direction's target out of range refuses the open with its reason, as `app_buffer_ns` does.
  - **ASRC seed:** each player's rate fit locks 60 s after an open; until then its rate term was 0, so a driver 7.5 ppm off Dante drifted the depth about 0.45 ms before the lock (dasio's measurement; the controller's own test reads 0.440 ms, 1.27 ms at 50 ppm). Seeded with the last run's locked rate the correction starts there (0.000 ms of first-minute drift in the same test). dstack-asio keeps the two locked rates in a small file the app names (`bridge::Seed`: `dante_rate`, `driver_rate`, `to_asio_ppm`, `from_asio_ppm`, `key = value` lines; written whole through a temporary file and a rename): `Seed::read(path)` at the start (`Ok(None)` without a file; an error for an unreadable or foreign file, to log and start unseeded), `Bridge::seed_from(seed)` before the driver's first open, and `Bridge::seed()` written with `Seed::write(path)` when it changed, from the app's own loop (a fit locks 60 s after an open). A seed counts only at the rates it was locked at, and within ±300 ppm (the controller's bound; a seed that is no number is ignored). A reopen at the same rates starts from the last run's own lock without the file; an open at other rates, until its fits lock, keeps the seed known before it (`Bridge::seed` never turns a known seed into an empty one). dlink: `Player::with_seed_ppm`, `Stats::locked_ppm` (the rate the fit last locked on, kept across a re-prime and the next player on the same counts). dstack-resample: `asrc::Controller::seed_ppm` / `seed` (the rate until the fit locks, and again while a stall has flushed it; the correction starts at it when given before the first observation).
  - `Bridge::with_clock` now also stamps the bridge's FIFOs on its clock (a simulation's time; `Bridge::new` is unchanged: the app's clock, the FIFOs' default).
  - **No latency change** for any consumer: every default is the same (`app_buffer_ns` for both directions, an unseeded start, the 256-tap filter). A dasio bridge moves only when its config sets a direction's target.
  - **Shorter converter options (#382, measured, not changed):** the table of 256/192/128/96/64/48/32 taps (delay, passband to 20 kHz, fold/image, THD+N at 48->96, 96->48 and 96<->96) is on #382 for the owner's pick; the bridge keeps 256 taps.
  - Each consumer:
    - **dasio:** `Spec` gained two fields: add `to_asio_app_buffer_ns: None, from_asio_app_buffer_ns: None` to its `Spec { .. }` (or set them from new `[bridge]` keys; dsg could run to-ASIO at 1.333 ms with from-ASIO at what its driver's lateness needs). For the seed: a path per bridge instance (beside its state file), `if let Ok(Some(s)) = Seed::read(&path) { bridge.seed_from(s) }` before the runner's first open (log an `Err`), and in the status loop `if let Some(s) = bridge.seed() && last != Some(s) { s.write(&path)?; last = Some(s) }`.
    - **dtray:** its dasio settings read dasio's `[bridge]` table with unknown fields refused; new dasio keys for the per-direction targets need dtray to know them before dasio writes them (to follow with dasio's key names).
    - **obs, camera-box, intercom, songplayer, presenter, iemmixer, dsend, dlisten:** nothing to change (the dlink/dstack-resample additions are new methods; a player unseeded runs as before).

## 0.6.171

- **Fix (#383): the start line no longer calls a Realtek driver version bad on any adapter.** The 20-30 ms receive-DPC measurement (dasio#1, the FOH PC, 2.11.2026) was on the 2.5 GbE family (RTL8125); dsg's 1 GbE "Realtek PCIe GbE Family Controller" on the same 10.79.20.1003 measured clean (17.11.2026), yet its start line warned. The verdict now reads the adapter's `DriverDesc` too: `lso::driver_of(provider, version, description)` keeps a measured version's verdict only on `lso::MEASURED_FAMILY`; elsewhere it is unverified. API (additive): `lso::driver_of`, `lso::MEASURED_FAMILY`. No latency change.

- **Fix (#307): an output callback held past its buffer no longer underruns and re-centres a pulled RX bridge (dlisten).** mbc's dlisten (v0.6.157) counted 71 underruns and 53 re-centres in 13.5 h, in bursts at the seconds its threads were held 19-46 ms by system work, with the packets on time. dlisten pulls its RX in the output callback (#307), so the held thread is the callback itself. A WASAPI shared-mode output fills the room it has (cpal asks for the buffer's frames less its padding) and its engine reads the buffer one period at each tick: a callback held past what the buffer still holds comes back to a card that played silence meanwhile, its pull reads the whole time held, and the call asks for the whole buffer. Up to now the excess past the call's take went over the controller's bound, which re-centred to the target after the call's first converter block, and the rest of the big call ran the FIFO dry (an underrun: re-prime, silence); a smaller excess stayed as latency. Now `Player::fill_pulled` drops the time the card played silence (`bridge::player::output_gap`, its own module `player/gap.rs`). The silence of a "dry" call (the whole buffer B: the ticks emptied it) can only be `p - B mod p` (mod p) plus whole periods p; a big call short of B had frames left, no silence. The call's excess is the silence plus its phase off the engine's grid, less the lateness of the call before the gap, which is measured against the least of the 4 calls before it (`Lateness`, started afresh after each settled gap; a pull that wrote nothing reads under the floor, as the next pull pays for it). At a dry call the silence that excess surely holds (rounded down on that lattice) goes at once, which keeps the depth under the excess bound; the next 4 calls add their excess, and the lowest running sum (the least lateness after the gap) plus the call-before's lateness is snapped to the lattice (`snap`: from a quarter period under to three quarters over, the noise being mostly late wakes after the hold) and the rest dropped. The usual call is the smallest that came twice in a row, the buffer the largest call. The drop is counted as the output's own gap (`Stats::output_gaps`, once a gap is settled or cut short by an underrun; `output_lost_frames`), and the output's clock counts the time, so the rate fit sees no step. Measured on a quiet dev1 (load 2-10; `dlink/tests/rx_pull_held.rs`: a pulled dlisten-shaped device read by a simulated WASAPI output, Windows' 1056-frame buffer for a 480-frame period, the engine reading a period a tick, an event held every 0.7 s): held 46 ms, before 10 underruns, 10 re-centres, 15480 samples off; now 0, 0, 0, 10 gaps dropping 13440 frames = the card's 13440 frames of silence. Held 20 ms, before 5 underruns, 5 re-centres, 5820 off and the wait +5.8 ms after the holds; now 0, 0, 0, 4224 dropped = 4224 of silence, wait 30.00 ms before and after. Held 25 ms with the 6 events after each hold 6 ms late: now 0, 0, 0, 4224 = 4224. **No latency change:** the target, P, the converter and the controller are the same, and the bridge's wait after a gap is the wait before it, within these bounds: the drop is a period off when every call after the hold is 7.5 ms or more later than the least lateness before it (or more than 2.5 ms earlier), and a buffer of 3 periods whose hold ends late in a period can still re-centre once. Not covered: a callback held after it read its room but before its pull (a call of the usual size with a large excess, which is also what a pull skipped on the call before looks like); that excess stays with the controller as before.
  - **dlisten:** its callback calls `fill_pulled` on Windows (`fill_with` elsewhere: ALSA through PipeWire calls follow the hardware position, no whole-buffer call to tell a gap by); the 10 s log line names the gaps (`, 2 output gaps (20.0 ms the output played silence of its own)`, the frames dropped for them) and the app status carries `output_gaps` and `output_lost_ms`: a burst on a held PC now reads as the output's, not the bridge's underruns.
  - API (additive). dlink: `bridge::Player::fill_pulled`, `bridge::player::{output_gap, snap, usual_call, Gap, GapState, Lateness, RECENT_CALLS, SETTLE_CALLS}`, `bridge::Stats::output_gaps`/`output_lost_frames`. dstack-endpoint: `status::Snapshot` gains `output_gaps` and `output_lost_ms` (a struct literal of it must add them; dsend's and dlisten's do).
  - **dasio, obs, intercom, songplayer, camera-box, presenter:** nothing to change (`fill_with`/`fill_at` are unchanged; a fixed-size callback such as an ASIO switch is no fill-the-room output).

## 0.6.170

- **Fix (#379): an RX channel no longer stays silent after the clock goes back to another time base.** On stream-obs (dstack 0.6.144, 16.11.2026) the media clock re-anchored 43.9 days back (a new PTP time base; the transmitter's stamps went back with it), and from then on every sample of both RX channels read as a ring hole and every write was refused late, with the packets on time. The jitter ring kept its furthest read on the old base (`Ring::read_at` only ever raised it), so it would have stayed silent until the new base caught up, 43.9 days later. Now a read that ends a whole ring (65536 samples, 0.68 s at 96 kHz) or more before the furthest read rebases the ring: it becomes the furthest and the flow starts again, so the audio returns at the reader's first read on the new base. The packets written on the new base before that read are counted `late`, the positions before the first stored write `start_holes`. A step back within one ring is unchanged (the overlap is re-read, its writes late). The RX flow's own bookkeeping follows the clock too: a flow whose furthest end is a whole ring past the clock's now starts over (its open holes counted as `gaps`), so its packets no longer all count `out_of_order` after such a step and a missing one is a gap again. A packet stamped a ring or more past the clock's now (a stray of the old base, or a transmitter on another base) is left out of that bookkeeping (its ring write counts `early` as before). No latency change, no API change.
  - **obs / camera-box:** move the version; an OBS Dante source then recovers from such a step without a restart. Its `rx_holes` (the rings' `holes`) no longer grows by the full rate after one.
  - **dasio, iemmixer, intercom, songplayer, presenter, dsend, dlisten:** nothing to change; any RX ring read (pump, app read, TX echo, dlink pull) gets it by moving the version.

## 0.6.169

- **Internal (#369): clock.rs/host.rs split, no change.** A pure move: the three process-wide spin switches (spin tail, bounded escape, no-yield tail) and their setters and readers into `clock/spin.rs`; the device's signals (`Signals` and the registered watch) into `host/signals.rs`. Every public name keeps its `dstack::clock::` and `dstack::host::` path (re-exported); no behaviour, API or latency change. Consumers: nothing to do.

- **Tests (#340): the info send-failure test tells the server's answer from its own packets.** After the reopen the test took the first packet on the info group as the answer to its request; the server's unsolicited board and product info (sent at each open) can come first (PR #378's coverage run). It now asks for the network info, sent only as an answer, and skips the rest. No code change.

## 0.6.168

- **Fix (#366): dlink's `wait_for_place` takes a test run's loopback place** (`DSTACK_TEST_LOOPBACK=1`), as `Link::start` serves there; outside a test run loopback is refused as before. No API change.

- **Tests (#376, part of #363): the TX pull tests name a starved writer thread.** `dlink/tests/tx_pull.rs`, `tx_pull_counts.rs` and dstack-asio's `tests/from_asio_pull.rs` failed on any bridge underrun with nothing to tell the test's own writer thread (the app's 10 ms writes, the ASIO driver's 256-frame switch) not being run from a stack fault: v0.6.166's main CI on windows-2025 lost 390 samples to one underrun with the TX side clean (0 holes, 0 writes after send). The writer keeps an absolute schedule and now records how late each write lands past its due time, and the bridge's underruns after it, under one lock with the window's own count (shared `dlink/tests/writer/witness.rs`); a write later than its least lateness since the start (the timer's steady oversleep, which costs no depth) by more than the bridge's room (the target less half the writer's chunk and a block: 5 ms for the 10 ms writer, 2.667 ms for the ASIO switch) is a stall, and so is a write still that late at a window's close. As in `to_asio_pull.rs` (#307), a judged window in which the writer stalled is set aside and the next judged, up to 4, with 100 ms unjudged between them (a set-aside window's silence still on its way); the last is judged as it is, so a run that stalled every window still fails. A failure prints `verdict RUNNER` (every underrun read within 4 writes after a stall, the stack's own counts 0) or `OURS`. The judged window's asserts are unchanged, and a window set aside loses only its audio: its TX holes, writes after send and the fill's restarts and faults are asserted 0 too (no writer stall makes them). Proof on dev1 (temporary edits, reverted): a 30 ms writer stall every 200 writes (20 ms every 400 switches for ASIO) read `RUNNER` in all three tests with every window set aside (4 underruns, each at its stall); one write dropped every 300 (one switch's input every 600) with the writer on time read `OURS` (6, 3 and 1 underruns, no stall before any). All three pass under `chrt -f 10`; plain at dev1 load 9-15 the dlink ones passed every run and `from_asio_pull` 2 runs of 3, the other failing `RUNNER` with every window stalled (the driver thread up to 9.3 ms late, before the floor was taken off). No code change, no latency change. Consumers: nothing to do.

## 0.6.167

- **Feature (#364, part of #346): an ASIO bridge's from-ASIO side on the TX pull (#363), so a late pump no longer sends silence from the driver.** Up to now dstack-asio's from-ASIO player ran in the pump's block (`DanteSide`): a pump block that woke within W and ended past its TX deadline left its stamps silent (`engine.writes_after_send`), and a wake past W re-bootstrapped and skipped blocks (TX `holes`), while the driver's audio sat in the from-ASIO FIFO all along. Measured on dev1 under `chrt -f 10` (`dstack-asio/tests/from_asio_pull.rs`: a bridge device pulled both ways as a dasio bridge runs, its pump running the bridge's pump side and stalling 10-20 ms about every 0.4 s, a driver thread switching 256 frames at 48 kHz with a constant input, W 6 ms, 8 s judged): before (the bridge's own pump side, `DanteSide`, playing the from-ASIO player on the pump, as dasio runs today; a throwaway run of the same test, 22 stalls), 20868 samples off the level at the receiver, 17920 TX holes and 22 pump blocks written after their stamps were sent (the RED's pump-run pull: 23178, 18688 and 23 with 23 stalls); now 0 off, 0 TX holes, 0 from-ASIO underruns, and the TX thread's own fill (status `tx_pull`) filled every block of the window with 0 restarts, 0 writes after send and 0 faults, both ways and on a bridge from ASIO only.
  - How: `Bridge::pull_from_asio` takes the from-ASIO player (and the switch that hands it each driver open's player) out of the pump's side into a `FromAsio` (dlink's `TxPull` + the block age's take), which the device's TX thread runs in whole blocks at the pump's own due time and stamp, just before it sends (#363). The pump's side then plays no from-ASIO audio. The modelled block age (#325, a diagnostic) is now taken at the TX thread's fill instead of the pump's block; the measured packet age (`from_asio_packet_age_us`, `from_asio_min_receiver_latency_us`) is the TX side's, unchanged. `FromAsio`'s `process` allocates nothing, takes no lock and makes no system call but clock reads (the age's, the player's FIFO stamps) (`tests/from_asio_alloc.rs`, rtsan-marked, the rtsan job's dstack-asio test).
  - **No latency change:** D, W, (c) TX, P, the bridge target (the ASIO buffer + one pump block, or the set `app_buffer_ns`), tx_margin and the advertised latency are the same; the from-ASIO FIFO is popped at the same time relative to each stamp in steady state.
  - API (additive). dlink: `bridge::TxProcess` (a `Process` the TX thread runs for pulled TX channels, with `outputs()`; `TxPull` is one), `Link::start_tx_pulling(_on)` now take any `T: TxProcess` (a `TxPull` argument compiles as before), `Link::start_pulling_with_tx(_on)` (the RX pull of `Link::start_pulling` and a TX pull on one device: the app gets the `RxPull`, the TX thread fills `map.tx`, the pump runs the processor on no RX input and no TX channel; a FIFO or pull that does not fit its channels is `HostError::Setup` before the start). dstack: `embed::Embedded::start_pulling_with_tx` (both claims). dstack-asio: `bridge::FromAsio`, `Bridge::pull_from_asio` (before `dante_side` and the driver's first open; refused for a bridge with no channel from ASIO, after either, or twice).
  - Each consumer:
    - **dasio** (port step, in `run_bridge.rs`'s start, which builds the device from dstack's parts; until then the old path, the from-ASIO player on the pump, works as before and moving the version alone changes nothing):
      1. For a bridge with channels from ASIO, take the pull before the pump's side: `let from = b.pull_from_asio()?;` before `b.dante_side()` (and, as now, before the runner's first driver open).
      2. Hand it to the TX thread before `prepared.start(..)` builds it: `media.tx.claim_fill(TxFill::new(Box::new(from), engine_tx.clone(), Timing::of(prepared.config()), prepared.config().encoding, Arc::clone(&media.rx)))` (`dstack::media::tx::TxFill`), the same timing and encoding `Pump::new` gets (from the prepared config, so a latency Dante Controller set holds). `engine_tx` stays the from-ASIO TX channels in `prepare_config`'s engine list (one per TX channel as now, each with its engine ring and `Source::Engine`). A refusal (`Refused { reason, fill }`) is a start error: the pull is inside the returned fill, so there is no falling back to the pump's path after it.
      3. Give the pump no TX channel: its map's `tx: vec![]`, `rx` as now (empty while the to-ASIO side is pulled, #307). One writer a ring: a channel in both is the bug #363's `engine_channels` refuses for embedded devices.
      4. Status: the device's new `tx_pull` object (`blocks`, `rebootstraps`, `skipped`, `writes_after_send`, `faults`) is the from-ASIO side's health now; the pump's `engine.writes_after_send` stays 0 (it writes no TX). The `from_asio_*` keys are unchanged; `from_asio_block_age_us` is now the age at the TX thread's fill. A dlink-built bridge does the same with `Link::start_pulling_with_tx` (to-ASIO pulled too) or `Link::start_tx_pulling` (from ASIO only), as `tests/from_asio_pull.rs` does.
      5. A refused second driver open now reads `the Dante device has not taken the last output yet` (`switch::NOT_TAKEN`, was `the Dante device's pump has not taken ...`): the taker is the TX thread when pulled. dasio's runner retries it as before.
    - **dsend**, **obs**, **intercom**: nothing (a `TxPull` argument to `start_tx_pulling` compiles as before; `switch::NOT_TAKEN`'s text no longer says "pump", as in step 5).
    - **dlisten**, **iemmixer**, **camera-box**, **songplayer**, **presenter**: nothing.
- **Tests (#373): the split test's drain bounded by its own step's time.** `stream::tests::split::each_drain_records_its_time_and_its_datagrams` bounded the drain peak since the start by an `Instant` around one `Pump::media`, but the peak also held the rig's own first drain (`flow_rig`), outside that window: at dev1 load 40-55 it failed 2 full lib runs of 55 (`3346 > 130`, `890 > 379`; the issue's `2983 > 102`). The test now takes the rig's second first, bounds the step's own second by the step's time, and pins the peak since the start as the larger of the two. No code change.
- **Tests (#373): the pump thread's play-time stamps bounded by measured real time.** `stream::tests::play_at::the_pump_thread_stamps_each_media_block_in_the_app_s_real_clock` bounded each block-to-block stamp gap by a fixed 33 ns of slew, but the play-time filter slews `SLEW_PPM` of the app's real time since its last followed pair, and wakes paced at about a block of real time read alike pairs, which the filter takes as a step. Each block now records the app's clock when it ran: a gap is bounded by the slew of the real time measured since the test's start, and a gap off that must be a step (over `REJECT_NS`, at least `STEP_WAKES` blocks after the last). A temporary 0.3 ms spin per block failed the old test both ways (333 367 ns; a 116 us step) and passes the new one; a pair taken at every wake (filter bypassed) still fails it while a wake takes far less than a block of real time. No code change, no latency change.


## 0.6.166

- **Diagnosis (#370, part of #339): each recent late wake carries its own sleep's oversleep.** Each entry of the status' `engine.late_wakes_recent` gains `oversleep_us`: how late the pump's sleep just before that wake returned past its target (the sleep's target is that wake), absent when unmeasured (a sleep asked for a time already past, a clock not read, a new epoch across the sleep). Before, only the second's peak (`oversleep_max_us` on the pump watch line) and the peak since the start existed, so a second with two late wakes told the cause of its latest one only: PR #368's ci-peer run 44032644214 had two re-bootstraps in one second (6979 and 7187 us behind, the line's oversleep 7192 us) and failed on the 6979 us one, whose own oversleep the gate could not see. Recorded where the entry is recorded: one more relaxed atomic in the ring's seqlock slot, no allocation (`tests/hot_path.rs`), no lock.
  - **The ci-peer gate** (`tools/linux/tests/ci_peer_gate.py`) matches each re-bootstrap the witness missed to its own entry's oversleep (past W, within 0.5 ms of its lateness; W from the wake's own late watch line), whatever else its second held; an entry without the field (an older peer, or a sleep not measured) falls back to the line's per-second peak under its old strict rule. Its unit tests carry run 44032644214's second.
  - API: `stream::wake::LateWake` gains `oversleep_us: Option<u64>` (a struct literal or an exhaustive pattern of it needs the field); `WakeLateness::record` takes the wake's oversleep (`Option<u64>` ns) before its wall-time closure; `PumpStats::slept` returns the oversleep it recorded (`Option<u64>` ns); new `Pump::slept` (the pump thread's call: records the sleep and keeps it for the next `Pump::media`'s wake). No consumer calls `record` or builds a `LateWake`; readers of `recent()` (dlink's soak, the peer tests) only gain the field.
- **Tests (#371): a granted flow carries an app's audio within 3 s, start after start (new `dlink/tests/flow_start.rs`).** SongPlayer's Windows CI (run 44030478973) once read a granted flow for 6 s without its levels, `live_flows 0` at the end. Not the stack: the TX sent the whole 4 s the flow lives without keepalives (11988 sends), the pump was on time, and `live_flows 0` was that expiry. The cause is in SongPlayer's test: its processor pops single samples from an interleaved stereo ring while the test pushes one sample at a time, so a pop between a pair's two pushes (the MMCSS pump preempting the test thread) shifts every later frame by one sample and swaps the two channels for the rest of the run. Reproduced through our stack on Linux with a 5 ms pause between the first pair's pushes: left -0.25, right 0.5, 0 holes, every packet sent. The new test runs SongPlayer's shape (a 48 kHz app on a 96 kHz device, two TX channels, a receiver of ours asking 32-frame 24-bit packets on loopback) 3 times on every OS and 40 times on Windows CI, and names what the receiver heard and the device's status when it misses.
  - **No latency change.** Consumers: nothing to do.

## 0.6.165

- **Change (#351): the #351 tail is now opt-in, default off (= v0.6.157's wait).** v0.6.158's no-yield tail (no SwitchToThread in the last 500 µs before a due time, a give at least every 10 ms) waits for its non-production soak, and every release since carried it. Now a device turns it on with the new key `spin_no_yield_tail = true` (Windows, the low-latency profile; process-wide, the last device set wins; accepted and ignored on Linux); left out it is off and the spin yields on every turn with no give cap, the same turns as v0.6.157. On Windows a device with it on logs `the low-latency spin's no-yield tail is on (dstack #351, opt-in) ...` at start, and the engine pump names its capped give (`the engine pump's capped give (dstack #351) is a yield|timer wait ...`) only then.
  - **dasio may move past v0.6.157 without the soak**: with the key left out its wait is v0.6.157's.
  - **The soak runs with the switch on** (`spin_no_yield_tail = true` in the soak device's config); a pass flips the default.
  - API (additive): `DeviceConfig::spin_no_yield_tail` (`bool`, serde default false), `clock::set_spin_no_yield_tail`, `clock::no_yield_tail_on`, `clock::no_yield_tail_line`, `clock::pump_give_start_line`. **Migration: a full `DeviceConfig` struct literal (iemmixer's `crates/iem-engine/tests/dante_loopback.rs`) needs `spin_no_yield_tail: false`** (or `..` from a parsed config).
  - **No latency change.**
- **Feature (#363, part of #346): the TX pull: the TX thread fills a bridge app's TX channels itself, so a late pump no longer sends silence.** Up to now the engine rings had one writer, the pump: a block's outputs reached the ring only when its processor returned, and the TX thread read each stamp once, when its packet was due (W + fpp + tx_margin after the block's due time). A pump block that woke within W and ended past that left its stamps silent (`engine.writes_after_send`, #346), and a wake past W re-bootstrapped and skipped blocks (TX `holes`), while an app whose TX audio runs on its own clock (dsend's capture, OBS's mix) had that audio in its FIFO all along. Measured on dev1 under `chrt -f 10` (`dlink/tests/tx_pull.rs`, an app writing a constant every 10 ms, the sender's pump stalling 10-20 ms about every 0.4 s, W 6 ms, 8 s judged): before, 14944 samples off the level at the receiver, 12800 TX holes and 16 pump blocks written after their stamps were sent; now 0 off and 0 TX holes. With the pull, the pump no longer writes those channels, so the proof of order is the TX thread's own count: `tests/tx_pull_counts.rs` reads status `tx_pull` and saw 0 restarts, 0 writes after send and 0 faults over every block of the window (about 3000 at 48 kHz, 6000 at 96 kHz with a 48 kHz app behind the TX converter), 0 off, 0 TX holes, 0 underruns. The release soak (`dlink/tests/soak.rs`, `soak/pulled.rs`) gains three `gate_*` cases on the pulled path in OBS's shape, the simulated TX thread waking as late as the pump: the pump's field late wakes and one wake past W with 0 TX tone breaks, 0 TX resyncs and, from the fill's own counts, 0 restarts, skips, writes after send and faults (the pump's path breaks the tone and resyncs once at that wake); and a TX thread stalled once 40 ms (past W and the lag bound) with one fill restart, one resync and its tone break only at the stall.
  - How: such an app hands the device a `Process` for its TX channels, claimed once before the TX thread starts. Each TX pass fills every block that is due, in whole blocks at the pump's own due time (media now >= kB + B + P) and stamp (kB + D, less the engine shift), then sends what is due; the TX thread also wakes at each block's due time. A TX thread that is late itself fills before it sends in the same pass; it catches up blocks up to W + the advertised latency behind, past that its fill starts again (counted; the processor hears a `discontinuity`, its player resyncs once). A start or restart begins as the pump's bootstrap does (the block due now, no catch-up), so a flow that starts or restarts with it sends W of holes first, as on the pump's path (filling those at once would pop W of audio right after the player's resync prime and leave the FIFO W under its target). A re-anchor moves or keeps the fill within W as the pump's schedule; a new latency P starts it again at the new playout point; a new advertised latency only moves the catch-up reach. A watch on the daemon's tick warns once a second when the fill restarted, skipped, wrote after send or its processor panicked (`TX pull restarted, skipped or faulted this second`, with `rebootstraps`, `skipped`, `writes_after_send`, `faults`, `blocks` and the panic's message in `fault`). The fill allocates nothing, takes no lock and makes no system call (`tests/hot_path.rs`, dlink `tests/tx_pull_alloc.rs`, the rtsan job's dlink test). A processor panic silences it for good and is counted.
  - **No latency change:** D, W, P, the app buffer (in steady state the FIFO is popped at the same time relative to each stamp; a start or restart is the pump's), tx_margin, the advertised latency and `Rated`'s TX converter delay and prime are all the same. The cost moves to the TX thread: the player's ASRC (and `Rated`'s TX converter at a converting rate), a few tens of us a block. dlink asks the app for no real-time setup.
  - A TX computed from RX in the same block (iemmixer's mix, any in-process mixer) cannot be pulled (its audio does not exist before the block runs): it keeps the pump path and `writes_after_send`.
  - Status (additive, top level after `tx_thread`, only on a device with a TX pull): `tx_pull` `{blocks, rebootstraps, skipped, writes_after_send, faults}`.
  - API (additive). dstack: `media::tx::{TxFill, TxFillStats, TxFillView, FillWatch, TX_PULL_WARNING, Refused, NO_RING, CLAIMED, BUILT, engine_channels}`, `TxShared::claim_fill`, `TxShared::tx_fill` and `TxShared::tx_fill_stats`, `stream::Blocks::with_margin`, `embed::Embedded::start_tx_pulling` with `embed::TxPullOf`. dlink: `bridge::TxPull` (the `Player` of the app's FIFO filling its TX channels, each piece at its own start; `TxPull::switched` gives the `Switch` that hands it a new player, as dsend's processor did; `TxPull::player` reads the one it plays), `bridge::Idle` (a processor with nothing to do), `bridge::tx_pull::{check, CHUNK}`, `Link::start_tx_pulling` and `Link::start_tx_pulling_on` (the TX channels `map.tx` from the pull, the pump runs the processor on `map.rx` only; behind `Rated`'s TX converter, with no RX side, when the device converts). An embedded device's TX channel named both in the pump's map and the pull, or past the device's, is refused at the start (`HostError::Setup`, "the app's TX channel N is named twice" / "is past the device's M TX channels").
  - Each consumer:
    - **dsend** (in this repo): moved: `Link::start_tx_pulling` with `bridge::Idle` and its `TxPull` (`dsend::source::Source` is now `dlink::bridge::TxPull`, `source::CHUNK` dlink's). Nothing to do by hand.
    - **obs**: may switch its Dante output's processor (a `Player` with `fill_at` in `Process`) to `Link::start_tx_pulling` with `TxPull::new(player, channels)` (or `TxPull::switched`) and the same `Player` arguments; the player's output stays at the app's rate (48 kHz) behind the device's converter, as now. Until it switches, it keeps today's path; moving the version alone changes nothing.
    - **intercom**: per its processor: a TX computed from RX in the block (the hub's mix) stays on `Link::start`; a TX fed from an app FIFO may move like obs.
    - **dasio** (dstack-asio's from-ASIO side): nothing yet; it moves onto the TX pull in #364.
    - **dlisten**, **iemmixer**, **camera-box**, **songplayer**, **presenter**: nothing (RX only, or a computed TX).

## 0.6.164

- **Tests (#366): dstack's own tests no longer multicast onto a site's network.** dev1's whole-device tests ran their devices on the box's routed address (its LAN leg), so each test binary announced mDNS there (measured: `system_clock` 780 datagrams in its 22 s, about 35/s; `embed` 73 in 1.3 s; `heartbeat_walk` 61 in 2.6 s) and sent its info and heartbeat multicasts with it; dasio found dev1 among the top mDNS senders on the FOH PC's Dante leg in the minutes its NIC stalled sends (dasio#127). Now:
  - **A test run** (new `host::test_run`): a process with `DSTACK_TEST_LOOPBACK=1` serves a device on a loopback address (`127.0.0.1`), with a locally administered MAC holding the address (`02:00` and its four bytes), no gateway and no link speed, and refuses any other address the lookup finds, before anything is bound ("a test run (DSTACK_TEST_LOOPBACK=1) serves a device on a loopback address only ..."), unless `DSTACK_LIVE_TEST=1` (an explicitly live test on a real network). The workspace's new `.cargo/config.toml` sets the variable for every process cargo runs here (`cargo test`, `cargo run`, nextest, cargo-mutants, CI). Loopback multicast never leaves the box; it was checked by hand on dev1 (Linux) and on a Windows 11 PC, and CI's ubuntu-24.04 and windows-2025 test jobs run the moved tests.
