A test runs our daemon as a device runs it for 2.5 TTLs of a short TTL (5 minutes of Dante's 120 s, scaled), hearing other devices' records, a controller's question and a subscription's lookups each second: everything it sends is an answer, an announcement or a probe for its own names (`tests/mdns_answer_only.rs`, which replaces `tests/mdns_refresh.rs`).
  - API (removed, vendored mdns-sd): `ServiceDaemon::{browse, browse_cache, stop_browse, resolve_hostname, stop_resolve_hostname, verify}`, `mdns_sd::VERIFY_TIMEOUT_DEFAULT`. No dstack crate called them; `ServiceEvent`, `ResolvedService` and `HostnameResolutionEvent` stay as types.
- **Feature (#319): the mDNS meter names a host that refreshes Dante records every 96 s.** The process's passive meter (#289, `discover::senders`) now also looks at what it hears: a host whose queries from port 5353 under a `_netaudio-*` type come on a 96 s cycle (a chain of 3 intervals each 96 s ±2 s, each point a refresh after at least 12 s of quiet from that host; other queries may come between; queries within 1 s are one refresh) is named, so a stale browsing program anywhere on the Dante network shows by address. A host that queries every few seconds is not named. A PC whose Dante Controller (Bonjour) browses Dante refreshes at 80-82 % of the TTL (96-98.4 s), so it is named only while three of its intervals in a row fall within the ±2 s.
  - Status: `mdns.browsers` (beside `top` and `over_cap`), each `{"source": "<IPv4>", "period_s": <its measured cycle>}`, a host listed while its cycle was seen within the last two cycles. Present (maybe empty) whenever `mdns` is.
  - Log: one WARN per host per hour, target `dstack::discover::senders`, message `an mDNS host refreshes Dante records every 96 s: a program there browses Dante, and every Dante host answers it at once (dstack #319)`, fields `source` and `period_s`, from the same minute report as the `an mDNS sender is over the cap` WARN.
  - API (changed): `discover::senders::Meter::start` takes a `discover::senders::browsers::Browsers` (`Browsers::default()` is the 96 s cycle); `Minute` has a `browsers` field. API (additive): `discover::senders::browsers`.
- **Counted (#303): a converter error in the bridge Player.** A block the converter refused played as silence with no count. `dlink::bridge::Stats::convert_errors()` now counts each such block (a later player on the same `Stats` counts on); the shared status `dstack_endpoint::status::Snapshot` gains `convert_errors` (the status line adds `, N convert errors` when there are any), and dsend's and dlisten's app status JSON gain `"convert_errors"`. It never happens with the sizes the converter gives the player; an app that publishes its bridge counters (obs: `tx_underruns` and its kin) may publish it beside them.
  - API: `Snapshot` (all fields public) has a new field `convert_errors`: code that builds a `Snapshot` literal adds `convert_errors: 0` (in this repo: tests only).
- **Test (#303): the soak gate ties every TX tone break to its own counted event.** From the TX bridge's first primed step on (the settle time and a fault's 30 s included, never only the judged window), each break of the TX output's tone needs an underrun, a re-centre or a resync at most 20 ms before it, one event a break (a converter error is not such an event: its break fails the gate); every case also checks that the tone check locked, and a fault case that its skip was heard; before, the two totals were compared from the moment both bridges primed. The judged window stays for the `tx_ppm` band. All 11 gate cases pass unchanged.
- **Test peer (#303): `dstack-dev peer`'s tone-break WARN carries `kind`.** `kind=silence` when the tone's energy dropped (the audio was not there, e.g. silence in the app's own mix: obs's 24 ms breaks of 15.11.2026), `kind=misplaced` when a sample was out of place (a skip, a repeat, a jump; also a gain step that keeps over a quarter of the energy, or a NaN sample). API change: `peer::tone::ToneCheck::block_with`'s callback takes a third argument, the new `peer::tone::GapKind` (`of`, `as_str`, `Display`; `SILENT_PAIRS`).
- **No latency change** for any device or dlink app: P, (c), W and the app buffer (the bridge target) are as in 0.6.144.
- Consumers: dcontroller reads `mdns.browsers[]` (or its WARN) to relay the 96 s alarm the way it relays `over_cap`, and `network.below_floor` (or its WARN) for the network floor (its own change, asked of it separately). obs (dante-input TX) may publish `stats.convert_errors()` with its TX counters and read the peer WARN's `kind` in its soak. dsend, dlisten: nothing to port. dtray, dasio, intercom, songplayer, camera-box, presenter, iemmixer: move the stack version; nothing to port.

## 0.6.144

- **Fix (#317, dasio, field 15.11.2026): an ASIO bridge no longer runs dry on every callback after a pump rebootstrap.** dstack-asio's Dante -> ASIO player took the ASIO buffer (512 frames at 44.1 kHz: 1115 frames at 96 kHz) as its producer's chunk, but the pump writes that FIFO 32 frames a block. So every re-prime followed a 512-frame writer schedule that does not exist; after a rebootstrap that skipped 38 pump blocks (12.7 ms) the schedule said "due now", the re-prime ended under one callback's take, and every callback ran dry and re-primed there again (`to_asio_underruns` +864 every 10 s for 2.5 minutes, 86/s). In a bridge simulation of that shape: 2583 underruns in 30 s before, 1 after (card ±20 ppm).
  - **API change: `Player::new(fifo, net_rate, out_rate, buffer, producer_chunk_ns, stats)`** takes what the FIFO's producer writes at once, ns, apart from the `AppBuffer` (the target's terms). In this repo: dstack-asio passes the pump block to ASIO and the ASIO buffer from ASIO, dlisten the pump block, dsend its capture packet.
  - dstack-asio's driver side fills the driver's whole buffer in one `fill_at` call (its scratch sized at the open, never allocated in the callback): the player primes for the frames of one call, and the 256-frame pieces would have primed it half a 512-frame buffer low (+100 ppm within 30 s at no drift).
  - The phased re-prime (a producer chunk wider than the output's call) never starts under one output call's take (`Player::phased_start`). A writer's count up by a chunk and up to a quarter more is a write on its schedule, not a burst, so a reader that looks once a callback re-anchors a small writer's schedule.
  - The soak's 11 gate cases pass unchanged.
- **Fix (#317): a device status read now and then shows the app's part as it is.** The app status hook (`Link::set_app_status`, `Media::set_app_status`) was called only while the status was read within 5 s, so dasio's status log (once every 5 minutes) carried the part made right after the log line before: `"stale":true` and `to_asio_underruns: 0` in the middle of a storm. The hook is now called once a second for as long as it is set, read or not; `"stale":true` means only a hook that has not returned for 3 s. An app's hook must stay cheap (it always was: a few atomics and a short JSON). API (removed, internal to dstack's daemon): `daemon::media::app::READ_WINDOW`, `app::Next::Idle`; `app::next(now, at)` takes no read time.
- **Test (#304): the one-minute real-time soak allows an RX dropout at a held app tick, as a TX gap.** An RX dropout may come once a tick at a tick at least 5 ms late or at the tick after it (after a hold over a period the loop runs the owed tick at once, and that catch-up read takes a second tick's worth back to back); at other ticks RX dropouts are allowed only up to the pump's own restarts, as before. CI 43745934559 (windows-2025) failed on exactly that. The TX rule is unchanged. The release gate (`soak.rs`'s `gate_*`) is unchanged.
- **No latency change** for any device or dlink app: P, (c), W and the app buffer (the bridge target) are as in 0.6.143. The to-ASIO FIFO's mean depth at no drift in the field's shape (512 @ 44.1 kHz, pump block 32) is 1301 frames at 96 kHz (13.6 ms) against 1286 (13.4 ms) before, within the sampling's noise.
- Consumers:
  - obs (dante-input TX, `plugins/dante-input/ffi/src/tx.rs`): `Player::new(Arc::clone(&fifo), obs_rate, app_rate, buffer, buffer.chunk_ns(), Arc::clone(&stats))` (OBS writes its tick: the same chunk as before, no behaviour change).
  - Any other app that makes a `Player` itself: pass the app's chunk (`buffer.chunk_ns()`) for a FIFO the app writes (TX), the pump's block (`buffer.block_ns()`) for one the pump writes (RX).
  - dasio: move the stack version (the ASIO bridge fix and the status log are in dstack-asio and dstack; nothing to port). intercom, songplayer, camera-box, presenter, iemmixer: move the stack version; nothing to port (none of them makes a `Player`).

## 0.6.143

- **Fix (Refs #303, obs's soak gate on 0.6.140): the TX bridge's level loop holds its rate under a steady app writer.** `Player::steer` read the FIFO's depth after each converter call (every 128 frames); OBS writes 1024 frames a tick, so the reads fell on a grid commensurate with the writes. With a writer as regular as OBS on an idle box (push gaps 21.34 to 21.42 ms), which reads came after a write stayed fixed until the write instant drifted across one read (~444 s at 6 ppm): the depth and the rate point held still (the rate fit locked at ~0 ppm under a 6 ppm skew), then stepped 2.67 ms and the braking pinned the ±20 ppm bound (obs: `tx_ppm` 0 for 2 min, then up to 39.7 ppm, two ramps 450 s apart). Writer jitter hid it, which is why the soak gate (±2 ms jitter) missed it; it fails the same way on 0.6.137's player.
  - `Fifo::push_with` now stamps each write on the FIFO's clock (the app's clock, `clock::app_monotonic_ns`; no allocation and no lock on either side), and a player called with block starts (`Player::fill_at(Some(..))`, every dlink TX bridge) measures the depth and rate point against the line through the writes: the frames at the last write plus the time since it at the nominal rate (at most one mean write ahead), less half the mean write. The writer's schedule takes the stamps too, and a re-prime on the line starts half a converter call higher, so a resync leaves no offset on the new measure. `fill_with` (dlisten's output callback) steers as before; every push now reads the app's clock once (vDSO on Linux, QPC on Windows), whoever reads the FIFO.
  - The soak gate has two new cases (`gate_ten_minutes_of_a_steady_writer_app_fast`/`_slow`, ticks within ±0.05 ms): before, the TX correction's 30 s median ran -20.26..-5.79 and -0.84..12.71 ppm; now -5.68..-5.33 and 5.88..5.95. Every gate case also checks that each TX tone gap is a counted underrun, re-centre or resync.
  - API (additive): `dlink::bridge::Clock`, `dlink::bridge::Stamp`, `Fifo::with_clock`, `Fifo::now_ns`, `Fifo::last_write`, `Fifo::read_frames`. A test that drives a TX player on simulated time gives its FIFO that clock with `with_clock`.
- **Test peer (Refs #303): `dstack-dev peer` logs each tone break.** One WARN when a counted RX tone break ends, target `dstack::peer::tone`, message `dstack-dev peer: a tone break`, fields `rx_channel` (1-based, the status's `RX n`), `samples` and `ms` (first bad sample to last) and `gaps` (the channel's count so far), so an app's CI can time each break against its own events. The status JSON is unchanged. API (additive): `peer::tone::ToneCheck::block_with`.
- **Feature (Refs #288, field 15.11.2026): the mDNS cap alone for a one-leg PC.** A PC whose one NIC carries both Dante and its LAN (the iemmixer box, the FOH PC) cannot take the full Dante-leg filter: its drop/Block rules cut the LAN. It now takes the cap on its own mDNS out only:
  - Linux: `tools/linux/dante-mdns-cap.nft`, its own table `inet dstack_mdns_cap` (one output chain, policy accept, no interface to name), holding `dante-leg.nft`'s two caps byte for byte (queries 10/s past a burst of 50, all mDNS 50/s past a burst of 200; a test keeps them identical). Load: `nft -f /etc/dstack/dante-mdns-cap.nft`.
  - Windows: `dante-leg.ps1 -Mode mdns-cap -InterfaceAlias <the adapter>` (and `mdns-cap-check`, `mdns-cap-remove`) create, check or remove only the QoS policy `dstack Dante leg mDNS cap` (200 kbit/s) and check the adapter's QoS Packet Scheduler binding; no firewall rule is created, read or removed. Idempotent.
  - `docs/two-networks.md`: "Which file fits which PC" (a separate Dante leg: the full set; a one-leg PC: the cap only).
- **No latency change** for any device or dlink app: P, (c), W and the app buffer (the bridge target) are as in 0.6.142. No producer API change: obs, dsend, the intercom and dstack-asio keep calling `push_with` and `fill_at`.
- Consumers: iemmixer and dasio (the FOH PC) load the one-leg mDNS cap themselves. obs (dante-input TX), intercom, songplayer, camera-box, presenter, dasio: move the stack version; nothing to port.

## 0.6.142

- **Fix (#313, dasio 15.11.2026): a device config error names the field.** A `device.toml` without `state_file` read ``device config: line 1: missing field `…` ``, and the operator could not tell which field. A parse error now keeps a backticked word when it is exactly a key of the config schema (`DeviceConfig`, its `[[subscribe]]` table, the `timing` names): ``missing field `state_file` ``, and an unknown key's "expected one of" list. Every quoted value and every other backticked word (a misspelt key may hold a device or channel name) is still cut to `…`. A misspelt key holding a backtick is now cut whole (before, the part after its backtick reached the log). The state file's parse errors keep cutting every backticked word.
- No API change. **No latency change** for any device or dlink app.
- Consumers (dasio, dlisten, dsend, OBS, intercom, songplayer, camera-box, presenter): nothing to port; move the stack version to show the field's name.

## 0.6.141

- **Fix (#312, field: iem-snv 15.11.2026): the LAN self-heal no longer stalls the PC every minute.** Its scheduled task started a new `powershell.exe` each minute; the NetAdapter/NetTCPIP/firewall module imports and CIM queries took ~2.4 s and ~1.6 s CPU (plus ~0.5 s in WmiPrvSE), and an audio device's pump on that PC woke late at exactly those seconds (:37-:41). `tools/windows/lan-selfheal.ps1 -Mode loop` is now one long-lived process from startup, at BelowNormal priority: each minute a quick check with .NET only (the LAN adapter's status and IPv4 addresses, the route's source address, one ping), no CIM or WMI. Only a failed quick check runs the full check, the down-counter and the repair, unchanged (never the Dante adapter, never a cycle of a virtual VLAN adapter, the threshold, the restart gap, the 0.6.109 cleanup). `-Mode install` stops and replaces the per-minute task (a start-up trigger, a 5-minute watchdog trigger with `IgnoreNew`, no time limit, restart on failure) and starts the loop; installing again is idempotent; `-Mode uninstall` stops the loop and removes the task.
- No library or API change. **No latency change** for any device or dlink app.
- PCs with the task (iem-snv, the SongPlayer PC, every one-NIC PC with the self-heal): run `lan-selfheal.ps1 -Mode install` again.
- Consumers: nothing to port; moving the stack version is optional.

## 0.6.140

- **Fix (Refs #303, field 15.11.2026: OBS on a stream PC): a TX writer late inside its app's pass no longer breaks the audio.** OBS runs a mix's outputs one after another on its audio thread, and a slow output ahead of dante-input's TX tap (an NDI output, ~10 ms) made the write land up to ~10 ms after its tick. The bridge `Player` re-centred any depth under its floor (min(5 ms, target/4)) as a dropout: ~22 ms of silence at OBS's 22.67 ms target, counted as an underrun and a re-centre (`tx_underruns` = `tx_recentres`, about 0.8/s in the field), while the FIFO still held audio and the write was on its way. The floor is gone: a deficit is a dropout only when the FIFO cannot serve the converter's next call (a real underrun, re-primed to the same target as before). An excess past the bound is still skipped. `recentres` now counts excess skips only.
  - The soak gate has two new cases in that shape (`gate_ten_minutes_of_a_late_audio_pass_app_fast`/`_slow`: one tick in 40 written 7.5-10.3 ms late): 98 and 174 TX gaps in 10 simulated minutes before, none now; all 9 gate cases pass.
  - API (removed): `dlink::bridge::player::FLOOR_S` and `dlink::bridge::player::floor_s` (no consumer used them).
- **No latency change** for any device or dlink app: P, (c), W and the app buffer (the bridge target) are as in 0.6.139.
- Consumers: obs (dante-input TX), intercom, songplayer, camera-box, presenter, dasio: move the stack version; nothing to port.

## 0.6.139

- **CI (Refs #305): the `ci-peer` gate tells a runner stall from a pump defect.** Main CI of 0.6.138 failed once: both peers' pumps (two separate processes) woke 4-6 ms late at the same milliseconds, one past the normal profile's 6 ms margin, and re-bootstrapped. The gate now runs a witness process with no dstack code (`tools/linux/tests/ci_peer_gate.py`); a window that rose is measured again (at most 3 windows, each void one a warning on the run) only when every re-bootstrap in it was a late wake inside a stall the witness saw too (other counters may rise with it: the other peer hears the restart); a rise without a re-bootstrap, any other cause, or an unwitnessed one fails. `docs/ci-peer.md` says how an application's CI can use the same witness.
- **Test (Refs #195): the Windows process-read test waits for its child's sockets.** Its powershell child prints `ready` once its 30 UDP sockets are bound (on 127.77.0.1, which no other program uses) and holds them until its stdin closes; before, the test polled for 20 s and on a loaded runner did not see the child.
- No library or API change. **No latency change** for any device or dlink app.
- Consumers: nothing to do; moving the stack version is optional.

## 0.6.138

- **Feature (Refs #195, field: the FOH PC 15.11.2026): a send refusal now names its cause.** The NIC refused every TX slot's sends for about 100 ms (WSAENOBUFS) during a service and nothing recorded what filled its send path. Each device now keeps the last 30 s of its NIC's traffic, second by second, on the control side's tick (`traffic::recorder::Recorder`: the NIC's sent bytes, this device's audio, the other traffic past our devices' audio, this process's and the machine's CPU; `traffic::cpu`), and at the first refusal of a burst (WSAENOBUFS 10055 / ENOBUFS 105, `media::tx::nic_refusal`, after 10 s without one; once a minute in a burst that never goes quiet) takes a sample of the part second that held it and logs one WARN:
  `NIC refused sends: the last 30 s of traffic slots=[1, 2, ...] seconds=-29s nic=<B/s> ours=<B/s> other=<B/s> cpu=<process>%/<machine>%; ...; -0s ... processes=<name>(<pid>) endpoints=<n> cpu_ms=<ms>, ...`
  On Windows `processes` lists the 5 processes holding the most TCP and UDP endpoints on the Dante NIC's address, this process and pid 0 left out (IP Helper, read once a burst off the media path; `traffic::processes`); elsewhere it reads "not read on this OS". The TX thread only raises a per-slot flag (an atomic; no allocation, no lock), also for a refusal a retry recovered. `docs/windows-box.md` says how to read the line.
- API (additive): `media::tx::nic_refusal`, `TxShared::take_refused`, the `traffic::recorder`, `traffic::cpu` and `traffic::processes` modules.
- **No latency change** for any device or dlink app: P, (c), W and the app buffer are as in 0.6.137.
- Consumers: dasio (FOH), camera-box, obs, intercom, songplayer, presenter, iemmixer: move the stack version; nothing to port.

## 0.6.137

- **Feature (dstack #305, requested by obs#57): a Dante peer and a drifting test clock for an application's CI.** A CI runner has no Dante network and no grandmaster; obs's release gate needs a peer to exchange audio with and the field's drift. `docs/ci-peer.md` is the recipe (a dummy interface, the counters to gate on).
  - **`test_clock_ppm` (TEST ONLY):** the TEST system clock (`host::ClockSource::System`, `--clock system`) runs that many ppm off the app's clock (`clock::app_monotonic_ns`: Linux `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, Windows raw QPC) (`clock::skewed`: `SkewedClock`, `TestClockPpm`, `MAX_TEST_CLOCK_PPM`, `media_ptp`; read lock-free), so play_at, an app's ASRC and the TX Player see real drift, and two processes drift against each other. A device config key (`test_clock_ppm = 6.0`, -1000 to 1000) and `dlink::Config::test_clock_ppm(ppm)`, which makes `Link::start` / `Link::start_until` start on the system clock. Any other clock source refuses a device that sets it (`clock::skewed::test_clock_refusal`, a setup error, exit 2). A WARN at start names the skew. An older dstack refuses a config file holding the key (`deny_unknown_fields`): remove it to downgrade.
  - **`dstack-dev peer`:** a TEST device on the system clock: `--iface <if> | --ip <a>`, `--name`, `--tone-hz <hz,...>` (one TX channel each, a -6 dBFS sine on the media sample grid), `--rx-from <device>:<channel,...>` (RX channels subscribed to the app's TX), `--test-clock-ppm`, `--ports`, `--process-id`, `--rate`, `--latency-ms`, `--state`, `--status <path>` (JSON, rewritten once a second: the peer's `tx`, each RX channel's `locked`, `tone_hz`, `level_dbfs`, `samples`, `tone_gaps` (a continuity check of any steady received tone), `play_at_ppm`, and the device's own status). Ends on SIGINT/SIGTERM, exit 0 after the goodbyes. The library side is `dstack::peer` (`Peer`, `Tones`, `tone::ToneCheck`). dstack's CI runs the recipe (`ci-peer` job, `tools/linux/tests/ci-peer.test.sh`).
  - **Status: `engine.play_at_steps` and `engine.play_at_max_dev_us`** (after `play_at_rejected`; every dlink device): blocks whose `Block::play_at` moved over 1 ms off their media time since the block before, and the largest such deviation since the start, µs (`stream::play_at`). `stream::PumpView` gains both fields.
  - API (additive): `DeviceConfig::test_clock_ppm: Option<TestClockPpm>` (a code that builds a `DeviceConfig` literal adds `test_clock_ppm: None` or ends with `..`); `PumpView::play_at_steps`, `PumpView::play_at_max_dev_us` (a `PumpView` literal adds them or `..Default::default()`); `dlink::Config::test_clock_ppm`, `dlink::Config::clock`.
- **Fix (Refs #303): the bridge `Player` resyncs when a dlink device comes back to its media clock.** A block without a start (free pacing: no media clock) cleared the player's next start, so the first block with a start after it never resynced. Now it does, once (`Stats::resyncs`), skipping no time (those frames were played). A start that goes back resyncs as before.
- **No latency change** for any device or dlink app: P, (c), W and the app buffer are as in 0.6.136.
- Consumers: obs: move the stack version and wire its release job per `docs/ci-peer.md` (its dlink `Config::test_clock_ppm` under test, a `dstack-dev peer` from this release's assets). camera-box, intercom, songplayer, presenter, dasio, iemmixer: move the stack version (a `DeviceConfig` or `PumpView` literal ports as above).

## 0.6.136

- **Release gate (dstack #304): the soak.** A tag gets its release only after `crates/dlink/tests/soak.rs`'s `gate_*` cases pass (`release.yml`'s `soak` job): dlink's whole app path in OBS's shape (48 kHz app on a 96 kHz device, TX and RX bridges, `Block::play_at`) for 10 simulated minutes a case, with the app clock ±6 ppm off, the pump waking up to 8 ms late, one rebootstrap, and an irregular writer (10-25 ms stalls with catch-up bursts). It fails on a TX dropout or re-centre after priming (an irregular writer: any gap away from a stall, or two at one), a TX correction whose 30 s median leaves ±(drift + 2) ppm, or a play time off the app's clock. The RX bridge under the field's 2-8 ms late wakes is not judged until dstack #307 (an owner latency decision); a case with wakes under 1 ms judges it strictly.
  An irregular writer's gaps are judged per stall (at most one a stall, none away from one) and its TX band is ±(drift + 20) ppm: at OBS's 22.67 ms target a stall over about 12 ms empties the FIFO whatever the player does. The fault cases must count exactly one TX resync, every other case none. The one-minute real-time case is its own test binary (`tests/soak_real_time.rs`, out of the gate, still in CI's test job): a TX gap is allowed only at a tick of the app's thread that ran at least 5 ms late (the runner's load), never at a tick on time.
- **Fix (dstack #303): the bridge `Player` stays calm after a pump rebootstrap and under an irregular writer.** Before, the TX correction swung to about -200 ppm for a minute after a rebootstrap, and to -50 .. +47 ppm under a writer that stalls (each re-prime ended mid-cycle, a chunk over the target, and the braking drained it at up to -70 ppm). Now:
  - **Resync:** `Player::fill_at(start, out, outputs, to)` takes the block's start (`Block::start()` in a `Process`). A block that does not start where the last one ended re-primes once to the target, skipping what the producer wrote meanwhile, counted in the new `Stats::resyncs()` (publish it as `tx_resyncs`). The output's clock counts the skipped time. `fill_with` is unchanged and never resyncs.
  - **The producer's schedule:** a chunked producer (its chunk wider than the caller's call, OBS's 1024-frame ticks) is followed on its own schedule (internal), so a re-prime starts at the depth whose mean over the writer's cycle is the target, whatever moment it ends at. A catch-up burst after a stall never moves the schedule; two writes in a row off it by the same error (a capture that stopped and started again) move it; while the next chunk is overdue by more than a quarter period, the player primes as before.
  - **A bounded level loop:** once the rate fit is locked and the depth settled, the braking and level loops move the ratio at most `bridge::player::LEVEL_BOUND_PPM` (20 ppm) from the fitted rate, so a level offset drains slowly instead of ramping. Before that (the first minute, a clock 200 ppm off), and while a stall has flushed the fit, the laws are unbounded as before. In `dstack-resample`: the opt-in `asrc::Controller::with_level_bound_ppm`, and the pure `asrc::bounded_level_ppm` and `asrc::settled`; other users of the controller see no change.
  - Measured in the soak (10 simulated minutes a case): the rebootstrap cases' TX 30 s median stays within -5.5 .. +7.0 ppm (before: down to -204); the irregular writer's within -6.4 .. +14.1 ppm (before: -50 .. +47).
  - In this repo: dsend's TX and dasio's (`dstack-asio`) both directions pass each piece's start (`block.start()` + the frames before it), so they resync on a skipped block; `dstack_endpoint::status::Snapshot` gains `resyncs` and the status line names `, N resyncs` when there are any (a code that builds a `Snapshot` literal adds `resyncs: 0`).
  - Consumers (obs's dante-input TX, intercom, any app whose player runs in a dlink `Process`): call `player.fill_at(block.start(), out, channels, to)` instead of `fill_with` (a block filled in pieces passes each piece's start), and add `tx_resyncs` (`Stats::resyncs()`) to the status. A player on an output callback (dlisten) keeps `fill_with`. No latency change: the target (`app_buffer_ns`) and every term are as before.

## 0.6.135

- **Fix (dstack #295 step 2, field: strih-snv 14.11.2026): `Block::play_at` no longer jumps on a torn clock read.** The pump read the media clock and the app's clock once per wake with no real-time priority (`normal` profile), so a preemption between the two reads moved one wake's offset by the preempted time: paired `play_at` jumps of ±1.3 to 2.3 ms that cancelled within 3 to 5 ms, about every 15 minutes, on v0.6.132.
  - The pair is now read app, media, app, and the media read is paired with the midpoint of the two app reads (`stream::app_offset::pair_offset`).
  - The offset is filtered (`stream::app_offset::OffsetFilter`): the first pair is taken; after it a pair more than 50 µs off the filtered offset is rejected (the offset kept), and a closer one is followed by at most 100 ppm of the app time since the last followed pair, so the real drift (about 6 ppm) is followed with no jump. A change over 50 µs read alike on 3 wakes in a row is a real clock step: taken on the third, with a WARN. A media re-anchor moves the offset by its known step (an unknown step starts the filter afresh), so `play_at` runs on through it as in 0.6.132.
  - Status: the `engine` part gains `play_at_rejected` (pairs rejected as torn, since the start), after `rebootstraps_retime`; `stream::PumpView` gains the field `play_at_rejected` (a `PumpView` literal adds it or `..Default::default()`).
  - New log lines: INFO once a minute in which pairs were rejected, `play_at: torn clock pair reads rejected in the last minute` (`device`, `rejected` in that minute, `total`); WARN on a step taken, `play_at: the media clock's offset from the app's clock stepped` (`by_ns`).
  - API: `stream::Pump::read_clocks(clock, app_ns)` takes `app_ns: impl FnMut() -> i64` (it reads the app's clock twice; a closure or `clock::app_monotonic_ns` passes as before), and the pair it reads stamps blocks from the next `Pump::media` on (as the pump thread and a pump driven by hand call them). `Block::play_at`'s meaning is unchanged. New: `stream::app_offset` (`OffsetFilter`, `Outcome`, `pair_offset`, `slew_max_ns`, the bounds and the two line texts).
- **No latency change**: one more app clock read per pump wake and a few integer operations; P, (c), W and the app buffer are as in 0.6.134.
- Consumers: obs, camera-box: move the stack version; camera-box's field check (A/V offset across restarts, no ±2 ms play_at pairs on strih-snv) follows. intercom, songplayer, presenter, dasio, iemmixer: move the stack version only.

## 0.6.134

- Internal: subscribe split into modules; no behaviour or API change; no latency change. (`crates/dstack/src/subscribe.rs`, 1204 lines, keeps the types and the public lifecycle; the RX publication, the resolving and requesting, and the flow-slot bookkeeping move verbatim into `subscribe/publish.rs`, `subscribe/request.rs` and `subscribe/slots.rs`; `subscribe::fpp_for` stays at its path. dstack #300.)
- Consumers: nothing to port; move the stack version when convenient.

## 0.6.133

- **Feature (dstack #297, requested by iemmixer#122): an opted-in device takes its own unicast flow over a coarser multicast flow.** A low-latency receiver (`arc_rx_fpp = 4`) received a transmitter's 32-frame multicast flow: 0.33 ms of packet time before the first sample left, so a vocal landed at 1.33 ms against a 1 ms limit.
  - **Opt-in:** only a device whose config SETS `arc_rx_fpp` takes part. A device that leaves it out keeps today's behaviour exactly: every multicast flow that carries its channel is preferred, and its unicast flow still moves to a multicast flow that appears (main's decision on #297; a band/FOH feed on the default sees no change).
  - On an opted-in device a multicast flow is preferred only when its fpp is at most what its own unicast flow from that transmitter would carry (`subscribe::fpp_for`, raised to the transmitter's 0x0314 step). A channel first joins the multicast flow as before; once it delivers, the device asks its own unicast flow at its fpp, only while the transmitter's TX flow list shows a free TX flow slot (its slot count less its flows; unknown, so not asked, when a record did not read). The request and its answer leave the transmitter's request wait and counts as they were. The channel stays on the multicast flow until the unicast flow delivers, then moves, and the group is left (make-before-break: no gap). A unicast flow never moves to a coarser multicast flow.
  - The transmitter's multicast flow is never created, changed or ended: the only datagrams are our own unicast flow's request and stop. A refusal, no answer, a grant that never delivers, no free slot, or a list without a slot count (ARC 2.7) keeps that transmitter's channels on the multicast flow while the device runs, with no failure counted and no status change, and it is not asked again (restart the device to try again).
  - New INFO lines: `RX multicast flow has more frames a packet than ours: our own unicast flow is asked for, the multicast flow kept until it delivers` (`tx_device`, `rx`, `multicast_fpp`, `our_fpp`, `free_slots`); `RX unicast flow delivers: its channels leave the multicast flow` (`slot`, `fpp`, `channels`); once per transmitter `RX channels stay on their transmitter's multicast flow: our own unicast flow <was refused | got no answer | it delivered nothing | is not asked: ...>` (`multicast_fpp`, `our_fpp`). The `RX multicast flow: a transmitter's multicast flow is received` line gains `our_fpp`.
  - API: `DeviceConfig::arc_rx_fpp` is now `Option<u16>` (`None` = left out, asks `directory::FPP` = 16 as before). A config file needs no change. Code that builds a `DeviceConfig` literal ports `arc_rx_fpp: directory::FPP` to `arc_rx_fpp: None` (the default, no opt-in) or `Some(n)` (opt-in); iemmixer's `crates/iem-engine/tests/dante_loopback.rs` has one. `subscribe::resolve::Multicast` gains `free_slots: Option<u16>`; `subscribe::directory::carried` takes the whole `arc::flows::Flows` list (it reads its slot count).
  - Field risk, measured not assumed: a 4-fpp flow is 24 000 packets/s from the transmitter; iemmixer measures the multicast's other receivers before and after.
- **Feature (dstack #298, requested by iemmixer#122): status rows name a flow's peer and our channels.** Additive JSON fields:
  - `tx[]`: `receiver` (the receiving device's Dante name, as its flow request named it; absent for a multicast flow and a slot without a flow) and `tx_channels` (our 1-based TX channel of each of the flow's slots in flow order, `null` for an empty slot; absent without a flow). Both come right after `flow` and `channels`.
  - `rx[]`: `rx_channels` (our 1-based RX channels the flow feeds, in flow order: each channel slot's in turn, one flat list), after `channels`.
  - A reader matching the TX row's text by prefix (`"tx":[{"flow":1,"live":`) now finds `receiver` between them; read the JSON instead.
- **No latency change** for any device on the default config or any dlink app. An opted-in device (`arc_rx_fpp` set) may now receive a transmitter's channels on its own unicast flow at its `arc_rx_fpp` instead of that transmitter's coarser multicast flow: less packet time, the same configured latency.
- Consumers: iemmixer: move the stack version (its `arc_rx_fpp = 4` opts in), port the `DeviceConfig` literal in its loopback test, and read `receiver`/`tx_channels`/`rx_channels` in its latency guard. dasio, songplayer, camera-box, obs, intercom, presenter, dcontroller: move the stack version only (a `DeviceConfig` literal with `arc_rx_fpp` ports as above).

## 0.6.132

- **Feature (dstack #295, requested by camera-box#1527): each block's play time in the app's own clock.** Across one OBS restart the stream's A/V offset moved by one OBS audio tick (about 21 ms): dante-input stamped audio by push time, so OBS placed the first block at a phase set by the bridge's sawtooth against its 1024-frame tick. A block now carries the time it plays, in the clock apps stamp with.
  - `dstack_audio_io::Block::play_at() -> Option<i64>`: the local monotonic time, ns, at which the block's first sample is handed to the app at the device's stated RX path, its media start + P + (c), in the app's clock (Linux `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, Windows raw QPC in ns: OBS's `os_gettime_ns`). `None` without media time (free pacing). Set with `Block::played_at(ns)`; `Block::new` and `Block::at` are unchanged.
  - The engine pump reads the media clock and the app's clock back to back at each wake (`stream::Pump::read_clocks`) and stamps each block `play_at = media time of (start + P + B) - (media now - app now)` (`stream::play_at_ns`). dlink's `Rated` (an app at another rate than the network) adds its RX converter's delay, so a dlink app's `play_at` is its start + P + (c), the RX path the device logs (`RX app hand-off (c) ...; RX path P + (c) ...`).
  - With the clocks running together, consecutive blocks' `play_at` are one block apart; a clock re-anchor steps the blocks' media `start` and leaves `play_at` running on in the app's clock.
  - API (additive): `Block::play_at`, `Block::played_at`, `clock::app_monotonic_ns()` (Linux and Windows; on Windows raw QPC ticks as ns, rounded down as `os_gettime_ns` does, never the steer model the media clock's own monotonic uses there), `stream::play_at_ns(start, timing, rate, offset_ns)`, `stream::Pump::read_clocks(clock, app_ns)` (a `Pump` driven by hand, as in iemmixer's tests, stamps no play time until it is called).
  - At a converter ratio whose app blocks vary in size (44.1 kHz on 96 kHz), an app block's first sample sits up to one app sample (22.7 µs) off the stamp; the stamps follow the device's blocks and never drift.
- **No latency change** for any app: two clock reads per pump wake (no lock, no allocation) and one field per block; P, (c), W and the app buffer are as in 0.6.131.
- Consumers: obs (dante-input): stamp each block pushed to OBS with `block.play_at()` as `obs_source_output_audio`'s `timestamp` (fall back to `os_gettime_ns()` while it is `None`); camera-box then checks the A/V offset is the same across 3 OBS restarts. camera-box, intercom, songplayer, presenter, dasio, iemmixer: move the stack version only (the field is additive).

## 0.6.131

- **Feature (dstack #288 step 2): Linux caps the Dante leg's own mDNS queries at 10/s.** The 0.6.129 cap counted queries and answers together at 50/s, so #285's querier (about 40 queries/s) stayed under it while every device's answers to it loaded the VLAN. Owner decision of 14.11.2026: queries get a cap of their own.
  - `tools/linux/dante-leg.nft`, chain `out`, a new rule before the 50/s one: `udp dport 5353 ip daddr 224.0.0.251 @th,80,1 0 limit rate over 10/second burst 50 packets counter drop`. `@th,80,1` is the DNS QR bit (0: a query); `nft list` shows it as `@th,80,8 & 0x80 == 0x0`. Answers (QR 1) never meet it and keep the 50/s cap with its burst of 200, so a responder's answer bursts (up to 28 in 100 ms measured) pass.
  - Read what each cap dropped with `nft list chain inet dstack_dante_leg out | grep 'limit rate over'`: the `10/second` line counts dropped queries, the `50/second` line the rest. Reload the file on every Linux PC with the filter (`nft -D dante_leg=<iface> -f /etc/dstack/dante-leg.nft`).
  - Windows unchanged: its QoS policy (200 kbit/s on all mDNS out) cannot match a payload bit, and a lower throttle would delay answer bursts.
  - Tests: the netns harness sends real DNS headers; a querier at 40/s for 6 s is capped to about 10/s (105 of 240 passed), an answer burst of 30 in 100 ms passes whole while the query cap is spent, and the 50/s flood is now made of answers.
- **No latency change** for any app (the filter touches mDNS only).
- Consumers: nothing to change; the filter is rolled out per PC.

## 0.6.130

- **Feature (dstack #292, requested by dasio#106): dtray's dasio settings window opens and edits a bridge instance.** dasio 0.1.83 makes an instance a bridge to a local ASIO driver with a `[bridge]` table in its `device.toml` (`driver`, `to_asio`, `from_asio`, optional `app_buffer_ns`); the window refused that file ("Settings not readable", unknown field), because it took out only `iface` before reading the rest as a dstack `DeviceConfig`.
  - The window now takes `[bridge]` out the same way (dasio's own key) and reads it with dasio's schema: unknown keys and wrong types are refused, so a typo is caught as dstack's keys are. Apply writes it back; only the keys that changed are rewritten, the table's comments and other keys stay (a standard or an inline table). The window never adds or removes the table: an instance's kind (DAW or bridge) is not changed there, and the elevated helper refuses a staged file that changes it.
  - A bridge instance's tab gets an "ASIO bridge" group: the driver from the PC's registered ASIO drivers (`dstack_asio::drivers`; a configured driver the PC does not have stays listed as "(not installed)"), the two channel maps as comma-separated 1-based lists (`1, 2, 3`), and the app buffer in ms (empty: dstack's default). Each needs a restart (↻).
  - Apply is refused, with the reason under the fields, for what dasio refuses at its start: no driver, a channel 0 or given twice, a map whose length is not the instance's RX (`to_asio`) or TX (`from_asio`) channel count, no channel at all, or an app buffer outside dlink's 1 to 150 ms. Changing the Inputs or Outputs count therefore asks for the matching map too. The window still opens such a file, so it can be fixed there.
  - A bridge instance's restart waits for no DAW and its banner names none: it hosts a local ASIO driver itself, so no DAW holds a dasio driver (`dasio::Daw`, `dasio::daw`; `Plan::release` is false for a bridge).
  - The read-back after an apply checks a bridge instance by its status's `app.driver` (its status has no `block`); the done line says the buffer, the maps and the app buffer are in the file.
  - API (dtray, additive): `dasio::bridge` (`Bridge`, `KEY`, `parse`, `write`, `from_texts`, `map_text`, `parse_map`, `buffer_text`, `parse_buffer`, the labels), `Fields::bridge`, `Field::{Driver, ToAsio, FromAsio, AppBuffer}`, `ReadBack::driver`, `dasio::{bridge_problems, status_driver}`, `choices::drivers` (takes the driver list read, or why it was not). `dasio::banner` takes a `Daw` instead of a `reshapes` flag. dtray now depends on dlink (all OSes) and dstack-asio (Windows).
- **No latency change** for any app (dtray only).
- Consumers: dasio: move the stack version; dtray then opens and edits bridge instances.

## 0.6.129

- **Feature (dstack #288): each host caps its own mDNS out on the Dante leg.** The generic filters (#264) shaped only which traffic may cross the leg, never how much; one program on a filtered PC could still flood 224.0.0.251:5353 (#285: about 40/s from one module), and every host on the VLAN takes it.
  - Linux, `tools/linux/dante-leg.nft`: a new first rule in chain `out` after the leg check, `udp dport 5353 ip daddr 224.0.0.251 limit rate over 50/second burst 200 packets counter drop`. Normal mDNS out on our PCs is 5/s at most. Read what it dropped with `nft list chain inet dstack_dante_leg out | grep 'limit rate over'`. Reload the file on every Linux PC with the filter (`nft -D dante_leg=<iface> -f /etc/dstack/dante-leg.nft`).
  - Windows, `tools/windows/dante-leg.ps1`: Windows Firewall cannot limit a rate, so install, check and uninstall now also manage a QoS policy **`dstack Dante leg mDNS cap`**: UDP, destination 224.0.0.251/32, destination port 5353, `ThrottleRateActionBitsPerSecond 200000`, every network profile. Unlike Linux's it counts bits and queues: about 50 datagrams/s of 500 bytes, 250/s of small queries (100 bytes on the wire), and a sender past it waits instead of losing datagrams. A QoS policy has no adapter condition, so the cap holds on every adapter of the PC. The install creates the cap before it swaps the firewall group (a refused cap leaves the group as it was). The check reads the policy back (one of the name, that shape) and fails when the Dante adapter's QoS Packet Scheduler binding (`ms_pacer`) is switched off; a Realtek Virtual Adapter (a VLAN leg) has no such binding, and the base adapter's applies the cap (measured: 600 datagrams left at 46/s on the wire under the policy). Run `dante-leg.ps1 -InterfaceAlias <Dante adapter>` again on every Windows PC with the group; `-Mode check` then reports the cap too.
  - Tests: the netns harness proves a flood over the cap is dropped and counted while announces, other UDP to the group and mDNS on another NIC pass; the PowerShell tests cover the cap with fake QoS cmdlets and read a real policy back on the runner.
- **Feature (dstack #289): who sends how much mDNS on the Dante leg, with an alarm.** A flood is named within a minute.
  - Each process runs one passive mDNS meter per Dante-leg address (started by the first device there, shared by every device of the process, ended with the last; nothing sent): a socket joined to 224.0.0.251 on the leg, kept joined across an interface change (`membership`, #282), counting datagrams per source IPv4 address each minute (at most 256 sources; a new one past that takes the quietest's place).
  - New INFO line once a minute, only when the group carried over 10/s in all: `mDNS senders in the last minute per_s=<all sources, n.n> top=<address> <n.n>/s, ...` (the 5 loudest, loudest first). New WARN once a minute per source over 50/s averaged over the minute: `an mDNS sender is over the cap source=<address> per_s=<n.n> cap_per_s=50`. Both target `dstack::discover::senders`, logged once per process; INFO at the start `mDNS meter: started on the Dante leg (passive, nothing sent)`, WARN when it cannot start `the mDNS meter is not started: no mDNS senders in the status`.
  - Status (additive, after `engine`, before `app`; absent before the meter's first minute): `mdns: {per_s, top: [{source, per_s}], over_cap: [{source, per_s}]}`: `per_s` every datagram that minute a second, `top` the 5 loudest sources, `over_cap` every source over 50/s; `source` is an IPv4 address string.
  - The meter counts the group as joined on the leg's interface only: Linux is told so (`IP_MULTICAST_ALL` off), and Windows delivers by the socket's own membership (checked on a two-leg PC: no LAN source in 20 s). On Windows its socket is bound to the wildcard address, as discovery's, so it may also take a unicast datagram to port 5353 meant for another wildcard socket there.
  - API (additive): `discover::senders` (`Senders`, `Minute`, `Rate`, `Meter`, `MeterWatch`, `Meter::open`, `Meter::rejoins`, `report`, `MAX_SOURCES`, `TOP`, `LINE_FLOOR_PER_S`, `CAP_PER_S`, `MINUTE_NS`), `Media::set_mdns_meter`, `Media::mdns_minute`.
- **No latency change** for any app (the meter is its own thread on the mDNS group, off the media path; the filters touch mDNS only).
- Consumers: move the stack version. dcontroller: read `mdns.top` and `mdns.over_cap` from each device's status and show the rates and the alarm.

## 0.6.128

- **Fix (dstack #282): multicast groups are joined again after an interface change.** On Windows an address removed and added again (`netsh ... set address`), seen with a new interface index too, drops a socket's group membership, and nothing joined it again: the device kept running and unicast ARC kept answering, but its mDNS answers from the device's address stopped, browsers lost it, and a receiver whose flow ended could not find it again (a two-leg PC, 14.11.2026).
  - New module `membership` (re-exported as `host::Membership`, `host::Link`, `host::links`): a watch per joined address reads the interface list (name, address, index) every 2 s and asks a rejoin when the address comes back after it was gone, or its index changes. Another adapter coming and going changes nothing.
  - The mDNS responder (Windows) opens its socket again, joined anew, and sends an unsolicited announcement of every published record (`announce::responder::announcement`), so browsers learn the device at once. Discovery (dtray's local list) and the latency listener leave and join their group again on the same socket. RX multicast flows: the daemon's tick asks the RX thread (`RxShared::rejoin_groups`), which at its next pass closes each slot's group socket and opens a new one, joined (its `RX multicast flow: group joined` line per slot); a join that fails there is reported (`RxShared::take_groups_missing`) and asked again at the next look. The info client opens a socket per request, so it needed nothing.
  - Known limit: a removal and re-add on the same interface index within one 2 s look is not seen (Windows lists the address again only after its duplicate-address check).
  - New INFO line at each rejoin: `multicast group re-joined after an interface change interface=<name> old_index=<n> new_index=<n> group=<group>` (for the RX flows `group=every RX multicast flow (joined by the RX thread)`, logged when the RX thread is asked); a failed rejoin warns (`multicast group not re-joined after an interface change; tried again`) and is tried at the next look. The responder adds INFO `mDNS records announced again from the device's address sent=<n> count=<n>`.
  - API (additive): `membership::{Membership, Link, Rejoin, links, rejoin, EVERY}`, `announce::responder::{announcement, Watch}`, `Responder::start_watched`, `Responder::rejoins`, `RxShared::rejoin_groups`, `RxShared::take_groups_missing`. `Responder::start_on` keeps its signature (no watch).
- **No latency change** for any app (one interface-list read every 2 s per watch, off the RX hot path).
- Consumers: move the stack version. dasio, SongPlayer, OBS, the intercom and dtray keep advertising and receiving across an address re-add on a two-leg PC.

## 0.6.127

- **Feature (dstack #281): why the engine pump re-bootstrapped, how late it woke, and what each media thread got of its priority.** SongPlayer's native Dante device logged one `engine pump lost or overran blocks this second ... rebootstraps=1 missed=5` (13.11.2026, MMCSS HIGH), and nothing could tell whether the pump was starved (and for how long), the clock stepped, or MMCSS dropped the thread.
  - Re-bootstraps by cause: `rebootstraps` stays the total; each one also counts in `rebootstraps_late` (the pump woke more than its margin W behind a block), `rebootstraps_clock_back` (the media clock went back more than `DISCONTINUITY`), `rebootstraps_reanchor` (a clock re-anchor whose step is unknown or off the block grid) or `rebootstraps_retime` (the device latency moved). They add up to `rebootstraps`.
  - The pump's wake lateness, on every OS: at each wake on the media clock, how far behind its due time the oldest block it found due was (the catch-up blocks after it are not counted again; a wake with nothing due and a clock gone back are no lateness). Kept: the largest since the start, the last whole minute's (ended by the pump watch once a minute), 8 fixed buckets (under 0.5, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50 ms, and 50 ms or more), and the last 32 wakes one block or more late, each with its UTC wall time, its lateness and whether the pump re-bootstrapped there. Recorded with atomics only (a seqlock ring); the wall time is read only for a late wake.
  - The media threads' scheduling, per device: each of the engine pump, the TX thread, its relay thread and the RX thread records its outcome and MMCSS's task index at its start. Outcomes: `mmcss_high` (in MMCSS Pro Audio at AVRT_PRIORITY_HIGH), `mmcss_joined_priority_refused` (in MMCSS, AVRT_PRIORITY_HIGH refused: the task's default priority), `highest` (MMCSS refused, THREAD_PRIORITY_HIGHEST), `sched_fifo` (a real-time grant on Linux; `realtime` on Windows, where it is a thread priority), `normal`, `refused`. A join refused and a priority refused after the join are told apart, in the log line too (new warning `runs in MMCSS Pro Audio, but AVRT_PRIORITY_HIGH was refused`).
  - Status `engine` (additive, after `max_process_us`): `rebootstraps_late`, `rebootstraps_clock_back`, `rebootstraps_reanchor`, `rebootstraps_retime`, `wake_behind_max_us`, `wake_behind_minute_max_us` (absent before the first minute), `wake_behind_buckets` (8 counts), `late_wakes` (wakes one block or more behind since the start), `late_wakes_recent` (`[{unix_ms, behind_us, rebootstrap}]`, oldest first, at most 32), `threads` (`[{thread, scheduling, mmcss_task}]`: `thread` is `pump`, `tx`, `tx_relay` or `rx`; `mmcss_task` null outside MMCSS; only threads that started).
  - The pump warning line gains `cause` and `behind_max_us` at its end (the message text is unchanged): `engine pump lost or overran blocks this second device=<name> rebootstraps=<n> missed=<n> overruns=<n> max_process_us=<us> media=<bool> block=<B> margin=<W> playout=<P> offset=<D> cause=<late|clock_back|reanchor|retime joined by +, or none> behind_max_us=<that second's latest wake, us>`.
  - API: `stream::Due::Rebootstrap` has a new field `cause` (`stream::Cause`, new); `stream::PumpView` has new public fields `rebootstraps_late`, `rebootstraps_clock_back`, `rebootstraps_reanchor`, `rebootstraps_retime`, `wake_behind_max_us`, `wake_behind_minute_max_us` (a struct literal adds them, or `..PumpView::default()`); `stream::watch::Grew` has new fields `late`, `clock_back`, `reanchor`, `retime`; `stream::wake` (`WakeLateness`, `LateWake`, `bucket`, `BUCKET_EDGES_NS`, `BUCKETS`, `RECENT`), `PumpStats::wakes`, `stream::watch::cause_word`, `media::tx::{Scheduling, Scheduled, MediaThread, Priority, MmcssError}` and `TxShared::scheduling` are new. **`media::tx::join_mmcss` returns `Result<u32, MmcssError>`** (was `io::Result<u32>`): `MmcssError::Join` is the old error, `MmcssError::Priority { index, error }` a join whose priority was refused. **`media::rx::spawn` takes the device's `Arc<Scheduling>`** (`TxShared::scheduling`) after `cpu`. dsend: its MMCSS log line names a refused priority apart.
- **No latency change** for any app (counters, logs and status only).
- Consumers: move the stack version. songplayer: read `engine.rebootstraps_*`, `engine.wake_behind_*`, `engine.late_wakes_recent` and `engine.threads` in the A/B/A runs; grep `cause=` and `behind_max_us=` on the pump warning. No consumer calls `join_mmcss` or `rx::spawn` (code search, 14.11.2026).

## 0.6.126

- **Feature (dstack #234): start holes apart from holes, the packets' write delay, and a minute line for each flow's holes.** On the editing PC's OBS device (13.11.2026) the holes were a start burst (3324 in the first 26 s, before any packet) and a few RX stalls an hour, yet the counters are lifetime totals and the wake delay (kernel stamp to the pass start) misses a stall inside a pass.
  - Start holes: a hole a ring reads before its flow's first sample counts as `start_holes`, not `holes`: one read before any write since the ring was built or restarted, or at a position before the least packet start written since, stored or late (the stamps before the flow's first packet, which never come; a flow late from its start counts holes). A wholly early write starts nothing. A channel's ring restarts when the channel is routed anew, or when its slot's flow is new (another transmitter or generation; a plan change of the same flow, such as granted or a latency, is none): `RxShared::publish` restarts it before it stores the route, and the RX thread again at its next pass before it writes, so an old flow's packet written in between does not start the new flow. Known limit: a new flow from the same transmitter whose old packets still arrive is started by them (the RX thread cannot tell them apart), so its first gap counts as holes. An engine output ring starts at its first `put_at`. **Ring `holes` no longer counts these**: a gate on `holes` (OBS's `rx_holes`, the sum of the rings' holes) now sees only a running flow's holes. The TX echo's `holes` still counts both (`Read::all_holes`).
  - Write delay (Linux, stamped packets): each placed packet's kernel receive time to the end of its ring writes, one more wall-clock read per packet that reached a ring, in a one-minute window like the wake delay's.
  - The minute timing line gets `write_delay_p999_us` and `write_delay_max_us` after `wake_delay_max_us`: `RX flow timing in the last minute: the RX thread's wake delay and the measured network latency device=<name> source=<address:port> slot=<slot from 0> packets=<n> wake_delay_p50_us=<us> wake_delay_p99_us=<us> wake_delay_p999_us=<us> wake_delay_max_us=<us> write_delay_p999_us=<us> write_delay_max_us=<us> network_latency_min_us=<us> network_latency_p99_us=<us> network_latency_max_us=<us>` (the message text is unchanged).
  - New INFO line once a minute per RX flow, only in a minute whose channels read holes (start holes not), target `dstack::media::late`: `RX holes in the last minute device=<name|-> source=<address:port|-> slot=<slot from 0> holes=<samples> written_late=<samples> rx_late=<packets> rebootstraps=<n>`. Each count is the minute's rise: `holes` and `written_late` the rises of the ring `holes` and `late` of the channels routed to the slot at the look, each channel's own rise (a channel routed in brings none of its past), `rx_late` the flow's `late`; `rebootstraps` is the engine pump's for the whole device (0 without an engine). The first minute after the start only learns.
  - Status (additive; totals unchanged otherwise): `channels[]` gets `start_holes` after `early`; `rx[]` gets `start_holes` after `late_at_read` (its routed channels' rings, summed) and, on Linux, `write_delay_p999_us` and `write_delay_max_us` after `wake_delay_p999_us` (the minute taken last; absent before it and for a minute without a stamped packet, as the wake percentiles).
  - API: `media::ring::Read` and `media::ring::Counters` have a new public field `start_holes` (a struct literal adds it); `Read::all_holes`, `Ring::restart`, `impl AddAssign for Counters`, `RxShared::take_write_minute`, `RxShared::last_write_minute`, `RxShared::flow_rings`, `RxShared::route`, `media::late::HoleCounts`, `RxMinute::holes_rose`, `RxMinute::rebootstraps_rose` are new. `RxMinute::watch` takes the engine's cumulative re-bootstraps (`Option<u64>`) before `device`.
- **No latency change** for any app (counters, logs and status only).
- Consumers: move the stack version. obs: `rx_holes` (the rings' `holes` summed) no longer holds the start burst; add the rings' `start_holes` if a line wants it. camera-box: the E2E holes gate no longer trips on the start burst; read `RX holes in the last minute` for each stall's minute and the write delay beside the wake delay.

## 0.6.125

- **Fix (dstack #278): a reordered RX packet is counted as reordered, not lost.** A packet whose stamp jumped past the end of the furthest one counted a gap at once; the overtaken packet arriving next was placed (the audio complete) but the gap stayed counted, and the loss watch warned `RX packets missing from a flow: ... never reached our socket`. stream-snv's two wire captures (13.11.2026: ~1.8 M packets each, 0 missing, ~3000 reorders up to 6 deep) showed only reorders.
  - A jump now opens a hole. A packet landing in an open hole fills it and counts the new `reordered`. A hole counts in `gaps`/`gap_frames` only once it is wholly behind the flow's playout point (latency + block), when it is the oldest of 16 open holes and another opens, or when the slot's flow changes. A real loss is therefore counted up to latency + block later than before; the loss warning follows it.
  - `out_of_order` still counts every packet that begins before the furthest end (reorders and repeats). A packet that fills a hole only after the pump read past it is `late`, not `reordered`, and the hole stays a gap (holes behind the playout are given up before and after each pass's reads).
  - Status `rx[]`: new key `reordered` after `out_of_order`.
  - New INFO line once a minute per flow with reorders, beside the loss warning: `RX packets reordered on the way in the last minute, not lost: each filled its hole device=<name> source=<address:port> slot=<slot from 0> reordered=<count>`.
  - API: `media::rx::RxCounts` has a new public field `reordered` (a struct literal adds it); `media::late::RxMinute` and `media::late::MINUTE_NS` are new.
- **Feature (dstack #234): the RX wake delay's distribution and the network latency, per minute, in the log and the status.** A dlink app without a status socket (OBS's dante-input) could not read either.
  - Each RX flow slot keeps a one-minute wake-delay histogram (50 µs buckets to 25.6 ms, plus the exact largest) and a one-minute network-latency window, both of the same packets: stamped (Linux, where the kernel stamps the receive time; nothing on Windows) and placed (our clock ready).
  - New INFO line once a minute per RX flow with stamped packets: `RX flow timing in the last minute: the RX thread's wake delay and the measured network latency device=<name> source=<address:port> slot=<slot from 0> packets=<n> wake_delay_p50_us=<us> wake_delay_p99_us=<us> wake_delay_p999_us=<us> wake_delay_max_us=<us> network_latency_min_us=<us> network_latency_p99_us=<us> network_latency_max_us=<us>`. Percentiles are the upper edge of the bucket at that rank (50 µs for the wake, 20 µs for the network), at most the largest. The first minute after the start is shorter and not logged.
  - Status `rx[]` (Linux): new keys `wake_delay_p50_us`, `wake_delay_p99_us`, `wake_delay_p999_us` after `wake_delay_peak_us`, the minute taken last; absent before it and for a minute without a stamped packet.
  - API: `media::rx::WakeDelay`, `RxShared::take_minute`, `RxShared::last_wake_minute` are new. The RX flow slots' counters live on the heap now (each slot's histograms are ~5 KB).
- **No latency change** for any app (counters, logs and status only).
- Consumers: move the stack version. camera-box: the loss gate now sees reorders as `reordered`, not `gaps`; read the timing line on strih-obs. An app that builds `RxCounts` literals adds `reordered` (none known).

## 0.6.124

- **Docs (dstack #264, #195).** `docs/two-networks.md`: how to read ufw's state before loading `dante-leg-notrack.nft` (`ufw status` or `ENABLED=` in `/etc/ufw/ufw.conf`, never `systemctl is-active ufw`), and how to tell an RSTP topology change from loss on a PC (several transmitters' flows losing ~40 ms at once on every receiver, multicast PTP intact, zero switch drop counters).
- **No code change, no latency change.** Consumers: nothing to do.

## 0.6.123

- **Fix (dstack #274): our ARC flow lists carry a flow socket descriptor and the frames per packet as a real Dante device writes them.** A socket descriptor (each TX flow's receiver or group in 0x2200, our address in 0x3200) starts with its length and address family, `08 02` (8 bytes, IPv4), as an ARC 2.7 card (Yamaha AIC128-D) writes it and netaudio reads it. We wrote `80 02` (copied from inferno's ARC server), which netaudio reads as a 128-byte descriptor in 0x2200 and refuses in 0x3200. The 0x2200 names descriptor's word at +8 is now the flow's frames per packet (the card states 4, 16 or 32 per flow); it was a fixed 0x10.
  - `arc::flows::v27::list_tx_flows_27` still reads `80 02`, so a reader on this version lists an older dstack device's flows.
  - No public API change.
- **No latency change** for any app.
- Consumers: move the stack version; nothing to port.

## 0.6.122

- **Fix (dstack #273): an info request the device does not answer no longer logs a line per request.** Bitfocus Companion's Dante module asks every device info opcode 0x100a once a second; no Audinate device answers it, and dstack keeps not answering it. Each request was one INFO line (`info request not answered`), about 86 000 a day per device.
  - Now the first unanswered request of each (sender, opcode) is logged once at INFO: `info request not answered (told once per sender and opcode, then counted a minute) op=<8-byte opcode> peer=<id> from=<address>`. Every request is counted, and once a minute the counts are one INFO line: `info requests not answered in a minute (peer id, opcode) requests=60 counts=peer 1 0x100a unanswered 60 peers=1=<address>` (ARC's gap report form, with each named peer's address). At most 256 (sender, opcode) pairs get a first line; all are counted, and a full set is cleared at the next minute's report so a new asker is named again. A pair is keyed on the opcode's bytes 26-27.
  - `InfoBuilder::answer` logs its own "not answered" line at debug now.
  - No public API change.
- **No latency change** for any app.
- Consumers: move the stack version; nothing to port.

## 0.6.121

- **Fix (dstack #271): an ARC 2.7 transmitter's TX flows are read.** A Yamaha AIC128-D (Audinate DCM, `arcp_vers` 2.7.41) answers the 2.8.9 TX flow list (0x2600, start code 0x2809) with status 0x0030 (op unknown). The ARC directory asked it every minute, counted `refused=1` in its minute report, and never knew the card's flows, so a multicast flow on such a card could not be found.
  - The directory now asks ARC 2.7's 0x2200 (start code 0x27ff, the usual page request, 16 flows a page, paged by flow id as 0x2600) when 0x2600 is refused with 0x0030, and asks that form first for the device from then on (and back to 0x2600 if 0x2200 is ever refused the same way). The flows read into the same `Flows`/`TxFlow`; the multicast lookup works as for a 2.8.9 device. A 2.7 list's `Flows::slots` is 0 (its table states a page's slots, not the device's).
  - A device's first refused flow read is logged at info (`a transmitter refused our TX flow list read (told once per device)`, with the peer and the error); later ones stay at debug. The success line `a transmitter's TX flows read through its ARC` names the list's `op`.
  - New public API: `arc::flows::read_tx_flows(c, form)`, `arc::flows::ListForm` (`V289`, `V27`), `arc::flows::STATUS_UNKNOWN_OP`, module `arc::flows::v27` (`list_tx_flows_27`, `LIST_27`). `subscribe::directory::Job` has a new public field `form` (a struct literal adds it); `Book::finished_flows` takes `Result<(Flows, ListForm), ReadError>` and returns whether to log the refusal at info.
- **No latency change** for any app.
- Consumers: move the stack version; nothing to port unless an app built `directory::Job` literals or called `Book::finished_flows` (none known). dcontroller may use `read_tx_flows` instead of `list_tx_flows` to list a 2.7 device's flows.

## 0.6.120

- **Fix (dstack #269): a channel whose record answered during a lookup no longer goes to the ARC directory.** The network resolver read a channel's record and its query's grace in two separate locks of the instance table. An answer landing between the two made that lookup find no record and no grace, so it asked the ARC directory: `arc.asked()` turned true, the daemon started the ARC directory's threads even where every transmitter publishes channel records, and one ARC read (3 requests) went to a transmitter that needed none. The subscription itself resolved one tick later from the record; no wrong flow, no audio effect. Found as a test that failed once on CI (`the_network_asks_each_channel_s_record_and_reads_a_device_without_them`, v0.6.119's tag run).
  - `Table::direct` / `Instances::direct` read both under one lock at one time and return `Direct::Found(Advertised)`, `Direct::Waiting` or `Direct::Missing`; `Network::lookup` matches on it.
  - API: `Instances::young` is removed (its one caller was `Network::lookup`; reading the grace apart from the record is what raced). New: `Instances::direct`, `Table::direct`, `subscribe::instance::Direct`.
  - New test `directory/tests/record_race.rs`: another thread keeps answering a channel while the test looks it up, forgetting its record after each find. Without the fix it fails within the first rounds (40 of 40 runs, idle, one CPU and load ~36).
- **No latency change** for any app.
- Consumers: move the stack version; nothing to port unless an app called `Instances::young` (none known).

## 0.6.119

- **New (dstack #264, asked by camera-box): `tools/linux/dante-leg-notrack.nft`, conntrack only on the Dante leg.** `dante-leg.nft` is stateful, so conntrack tracked every flow in the network namespace, on every NIC, multi-Gb/s NDI and JPEG XS included. The new file holds one table, `inet dstack_dante_leg_notrack`, with two chains at raw priority: `iifname != $dante_leg notrack` (prerouting) and `oifname != $dante_leg notrack` (output). Load it after `dante-leg.nft` with the same `-D dante_leg=<iface>`; a load without `-D` fails, a reload replaces the table.
  - **Only on a PC with no NAT and no other stateful firewall** (camera-box's stream and editing PCs). Never on a PC with Docker, libvirt or any masquerade/snat/dnat rule (an untracked packet is never NATed), nor with ufw or firewalld active (their `established` rules see the PC's own replies as untracked). `dante-leg.nft` itself is unchanged except for its header's error text (`unknown identifier`).
  - CI loads the new file in a private network namespace: it reloads idempotently, refuses a load without `-D`, untracks loopback and keeps a dummy leg tracked.
  - `oifname` in the output hook is reliable: the kernel picks the route before the hook runs, so no `fib` lookup is needed.
- **No latency change** for any app; no code change.
- Consumers: nothing to port. camera-box loads the new file on its stream and editing PCs (docs/two-networks.md).

## 0.6.118

- **New (dstack #266, asked by dasio): RX flows name their transmitter.** On mbc, slots 12, 14 and 15 lost packets together in short bursts and the lines named only the slot (0-based) and a peer index.
  - Status `rx[]`, two new keys: `device`, the transmitter's Dante name as the subscription that asked for the flow writes it (absent for a slot without a flow now; `-` for a subscription without a device name), and `source`, the transmitter's `address:port` the flow's last packet came from (absent before a packet, and again once the RX thread takes a new flow in the slot).
  - The loss warning names both next to the slot: `RX packets missing from a flow: ... never reached our socket device=<name> source=<address:port> slot=12 gaps=... frames=... socket_drops=...` (`-` when unknown).
  - The late-packet line names the flow's other end, its receiver: `TX packets older than the latency their receiver plays at ... receiver=<name> dest=<address:port> slot=...` (`receiver=-` for a multicast flow, whose `dest` is the group).
  - `RX flow requested`, `RX flow granted` / `refused` / `request had no answer`, `RX flow stop sent` and `an RX flow stop was not answered` add `device` (and `to`, the transmitter's flow-control address, on the request and the stop lines). An answer to a flow no longer in its slot reads `device=-`.
  - The RX thread stays allocation-free: it publishes the source in one atomic per slot, written only when it changes; names are read on the watch and status threads only.
  - New public API: `RxShared::source(slot)`, `FlowView::device`, `Manager::flow_device(slot)`. `RxWatch::watch` takes a third argument, the slot's device name (`Fn(usize) -> Option<String>`); `FlowView` has a new public field (a struct literal adds it).
- **No latency change** for any app: diagnostics only.
- Consumers: move the stack version; nothing to port for an app that uses dlink or the daemon. A reader of the status JSON gets two additive keys.

## 0.6.117

- **Behaviour change (dstack #264, the owner's decision of 13.11.2026): fixed flow ports by default.** Every device binds its RX and TX flow sockets on fixed ports from its slot (its ARC port - 4440, the slot it already picks; up to 32 on one address): RX on `29800 + 64k` .. +31, TX on the next 32, all inside one dstack flow range **29800-31847**. Before, an unset `rx_port_base` / `tx_port_base` meant ports the OS chose (ephemeral), which no filter can name. So any number of our apps on one PC (dasio, dlink apps, dlisten, dsend) are accepted by one generic Dante-leg filter, with no per-device edit.
  - Why 29800, not 30000: dsync holds UDP 31900 (its time server) and 31910 on the wildcard address on every PC, and 30000-32047 covers both; on Linux a bind beside a wildcard socket fails, on Windows it takes the unicast. The range lies below both OSes' ephemeral ports (Linux 32768-60999 on the three Linux PCs read; Windows 49152-65535 and `netsh int ipv4 show excludedportrange protocol=udp` empty on the four Windows PCs read, 13.11.2026, recorded on #264) and holds the bases configured before (30000-30031, 30100-30131), so those devices stay accepted until their keys are removed.
  - A configured base still wins. One whose 32 ports leave 29800-31847 gives a start WARN: `rx_port_base: its ports 47000-47031 leave the dstack flow range 29800-31847, which the generic Dante-leg filter accepts; ...`. One inside the range that is not the device's own block gives another: `rx_port_base: its ports 30000-30031 are another slot's block, not this device's own (29800-29831); a device of ours in that slot on this address cannot start: remove the key ...` (30000 lies on slot 3's block, 30100 on slots 4 and 5's).
  - A device whose ARC port is outside 4440-4471 with a base unset **refuses to start** (setup error, exit 2): `rx_port_base: unset, and the ARC port N is no device slot's (4440-4471), so the flow ports have no default; set rx_port_base and tx_port_base inside the dstack flow range 29800-31847 ...`. It never binds the OS's ports. RX and TX blocks that overlap are refused too.
  - The start logs the slot and ports: `the device's flow ports: fixed, ... slot=Some(k) rx_ports="a-b" tx_ports="c-d"`.
  - Removed: 0.6.116's WARN `rx_port_base unset: the OS chose this device's flow ports ...` (and the `tx_port_base` one): no started device leaves a base unset any more.
  - Fix: a device stopped and started again in the same process (a dlink app restarting its `Link`) could hold its TX flow sockets up to a second after the stop: the receivers' latency reader's drop did not wait for its thread. It now wakes and joins it, so the fixed ports are free when the stop returns.
  - New public API: `dstack::device::ports` (`FLOW_PORT_FIRST`, `FLOW_PORT_LAST`, `SLOT_STRIDE`, `TX_OFFSET`, `PORT_SLOTS`, `slot_of`, `slot_bases`, `flow_bases`, `FlowBases`). `Media::new` is unchanged (a caller that builds the media side itself, a test, still gets the OS's ports for an unset base); the host sets both bases first.
- **New: the generic Dante-leg filter**, one for every PC, never edited per device or direction (`docs/two-networks.md`): `tools/linux/dante-leg.nft` (one `inet dstack_dante_leg` table, `nft -D dante_leg=<iface> -f`, reloaded in one transaction) and `tools/windows/dante-leg.ps1` (the rule group `dstack Dante leg` on the Dante adapter, `-RemoveGroup dstack155` replaces the per-box group; `-Mode check`, `-Mode uninstall`). Both accept established/related, IGMP, ICMP, PTP 319/320, multicast audio 4321, every slot's ARC 4440-4471, flow control 4455-4486 and CMC 8800-8831, mDNS 5353, info 8700-8708, Audinate's unicast audio 14336-14591, the dstack flow range, Dante's groups (224.0.0.0/24, 224.0.1.129-132, 239.0.0.0/8), mDNS answers from 5353 to the OS's ephemeral ports only, and TCP 22 and 8092 (#239); Linux also DHCP answers (the Windows leg is static: Windows' services host, the DHCP client included, is blocked on the leg). Everything else on the leg is dropped. The Windows install creates the new rules before it removes any old one, so a failure part way leaves the PC's rules as they were. `crates/dstack/tests/dante_leg.rs` checks both files against the ports the code binds; CI loads the nft file in a network namespace (new `linux-tools` job) and the Windows group on the runner's firewall (disabled copy).
- **No latency change** for any app: only the port a flow socket binds changes, no timing, buffer or path.
- Consumers: move the stack version, restart the app, then move the PC to the generic filter (`docs/two-networks.md`, "Moving a PC to the generic filter"): camera-box (stream and editing PCs; obs and the intercom with it), presenter and dcontroller (the presenter PC: replaces `dante-guard`), songplayer (the SongPlayer PCs: `dante-leg.ps1 -RemoveGroup dstack155`), dasio and iemmixer (dasio runs the script on its PCs), dev1 (airuleset: replaces `dante_guard`). Remove `rx_port_base` / `tx_port_base` from every config (the default is the device's own block; a kept one gives a WARN); a config with an ARC port outside 4440-4471 must set both, or the device does not start. No code change for a dlink app. On a Linux PC running avahi-daemon, set `deny-interfaces=<the Dante interface>`.

## 0.6.116

- New config key `tx_port_base` (dstack #262; dlink: `Config::tx_port_base(base)`): the TX flow sockets take fixed ports, slot `n` on `tx_port_base + n` over the device's flow slots (32 at most), so a Dante-leg filter that drops unlisted new UDP (stateless, or with conntrack: the keepalives come from a port the audio never went to) can accept a receiver's keepalives to them. Unset, the OS chooses the ports as before: no default change. On the stream PC (13.11.2026) the keepalives to ephemeral TX ports were dropped by such a filter, the TX flow expired after 4 s and the receiver asked again every ~9 s.
  - Refused at start, with a problem line that names the clash (`tx_port_base: 1024 to 65504, its 32 ports clear of ...; 4409-4440 overlaps every slot's ARC ports (4440-4471)`): a range below 1024 or past 65535, or one that overlaps `rx_port_base`'s 32 ports, the device's own ARC/CMC/flow-control ports, any slot's ARC (4440-4471), flow-control (4455-4486) or CMC (8800-8831) ports, mDNS 5353 or the info ports 8700/8702/8708. A taken port is named (`TX flow port N: ...`).
  - The bound TX ports are logged at start (`TX flow sockets bound ports=<first>-<last> fixed=true`, or each port when the OS chose them).
  - With only `tx_port_base` set, the TX sockets bind before the RX ones (the RX ports the OS chooses could otherwise take a fixed TX port); a fixed `rx_port_base` still binds first.
  - New `TxShared::ports()`: each flow slot's socket's port.
  - New WARN at start, once per direction that has channels and no fixed base: `rx_port_base unset: the OS chose this device's flow ports, which a Dante-leg filter cannot name (...)` (and the same for `tx_port_base`). The same failure hit a receiver's RX side on 13.11.2026 (its RX flow ports ephemeral, the filter accepting 30000-30199 only, the audio dropped). A device without such a filter can ignore it.
  - `docs/two-networks.md`: why a filter cannot name the OS's flow ports and conntrack does not cover them, and the two keys as the interim fix. The standard comes with dstack #264: fixed flow ports by default per device slot inside one dstack range, and one generic Dante-leg filter for every PC.
- Consumers: additive, nothing to port. `DeviceConfig` has a new public field `tx_port_base` (a full struct literal adds it or `..`; `from_toml` and dlink need nothing). No latency change for any app. Fixed ports by default (no key to set) and the generic filter follow in dstack #264.

## 0.6.115

- Fix (dstack #239, `tools/windows/lan-selfheal.ps1`): the LAN self-heal never disables, enables or restarts a virtual VLAN adapter. `Disable-NetAdapter` on a Realtek Virtual Adapter (driver 10.4.1117.2020) bugchecked dasio's test PC, and the repair's `Restart-NetAdapter` (a disable plus an enable) would have done the same on a layout-B PC, whose LAN adapter is that virtual adapter. A pure `Test-VirtualVlanAdapter` reads the LAN adapter's `InterfaceDescription` ("Realtek Virtual Adapter", or one naming a VLAN or a vendor's virtual adapter); for such an adapter the repair is the VLAN ID (`-NoRestart`), the IPv4 binding, DHCP, then `RenewDHCPLease` on that adapter (by interface index), and the log says the restart was skipped. A disabled virtual LAN adapter is a WARN and exit 1, never enabled. A VLAN ID the repair had to write to a virtual adapter (`-NoRestart`) may apply only when the adapter re-initializes: a WARN (reboot the PC if the LAN stays down) and exit 1. A LAN adapter whose description cannot be read fails closed: repaired like a virtual one, with a WARN. The Enable and Restart steps also refuse a virtual or undescribed adapter themselves. Layout A (the LAN on the physical base adapter) keeps its restart, as tested on the SongPlayer PC.
- Change (lan-selfheal): the state file is written to a `.tmp` file with write-through and swapped in (`File.Replace`), so a crash leaves the old or the new file; one that is unreadable all the same (NUL bytes) is still logged and counted from 0, now tested. The install's log line names the kind of LAN adapter.
